### 5. Retrieval side: are the 100 representative? (analysis 5; descriptive, `rq3_pilot_retrieval.csv`)

Share of gold files in context (mean) / share of queries with no gold file, pilot (n 100) against dev matched queries (n 353, `units_all`), BM25: 66.0 / 19.0% [12, 27] against 63.3 / 21.0%; RRF: 75.1 / 9.0% [4, 15] against 70.0 / 13.9%. Unit level (`units_all_nomod`, mean share of gold units): pilot 29.3 / 34.6 against dev 26.0 / 29.6. The pilot is level with dev for BM25 and a little easier for RRF, inside the pilot's interval. **Against the 377 executable instances (D108, L2, 8,000)** the pilot is easier: no gold file in the context 19.0 / 9.0% (BM25 / RRF) against 30.6 / 19.7%; gold unit present 68 / 83 against 51.9 / 64.1%. Estimate, not a measurement: the pilot's within-stratum unusable shares reweighted to the executable subset's coverage give a first-reply unusable share of 32.5% (file strata) to 34.7% (unit strata) for BM25 and 24.2% to 28.4% for RRF, after follow-ups 22.5 to 23.7% and 12.1 to 15.9% (`rq3_pilot_extrapolation_executable.csv`). BM25 would then sit at the one-third line of D104 on the executable subset.

### 6. Cost of the full runs (analysis 6; all figures estimated from the measured rates; `rq3_pilot_cost_grids.csv`)

Per arm-run (377 instances): 533 calls [487, 581] (1.41 per unit times 377), 4.76 million input and 0.35 million output tokens. Requests per day, not tokens per minute, bind: at 13 requests a minute the tokens are 116,000 a minute against the effective limit of 225,000 (52%); at B = 16,000 (input about 17,000 tokens per call, estimate) 13 a minute would be about 220,000, just inside. Calendar days = calls divided by 450 (500 nominal) rounded up, intervals from the calls interval:

| arm-runs | calls | days at 450 a day | days at 500 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 533 | 2 | 2 (1 to 2) |
| 2 | 1,065 | 3 | 3 (2 to 3) |
| 3 | 1,598 | 4 | 4 (3 to 4) |
| 4 | 2,130 | 5 (5 to 6) | 5 (4 to 5) |
| 6 | 3,195 | 8 (7 to 8) | 7 (6 to 7) |

Grids: RAW against the untrained encoder (D80 fallback), 2 arms x 1 run 3 days, x 2 runs 5, x 3 runs 8; that fallback with BM25 and RRF reference arms (4 arms): x 1 run 5 days (5 to 6), x 2 runs 10 (9 to 11), x 3 runs 15 (13 to 16). Sensitivity (estimate): if the executable subset's higher unusable share (analysis 5) raises BM25's calls per unit to about 1.6 to 1.7, the totals rise by under 10%, which moves a grid by at most one day up to 8 arm-runs. Grading is separate: one pass over the 377 is 3.7 to 4.0 CPU-hours (D106, estimate), so 8 arm-runs is about 30 CPU-hours.

### 7. Power update (analysis 7; `rq3_pilot_power_context.csv`)

The pilot gives **no resolved-rate discordance**; nothing was graded. At n = 377 the placeholder table stays: minimum detectable difference 4.75 / 5.75 / 6.75 / 8.25 points at discordance 10 / 15 / 20 / 30%. Shown beside it as context, and **not the same quantity**: the pilot's paired discordance between BM25 and RRF on the same instance and run (200 pairs) is 17.5% for usability after follow-ups, 21.0% for usability of the first reply, 12.0% for a gold file in the context and 17.0% for a gold unit in the context. The placeholders are not replaced.

### Decisions

1. **The free tier remains workable for the RQ3 runs as designed.** All three criteria pass (1.41 calls per unit, 22.5% first-reply unusable, cap hit with 311 units final); 2 to 8 arm-runs need 3 to 8 days and the 4-arm fallback grid 5 to 15 days. Cautions: BM25's estimated first-reply share on the executable subset is at the one-third line; the limits and the model are not guaranteed over 5 to 15 days (re-read the limits and the model string at the start and the end, as the log records it per call). The paid fallback stays as ADR-008 declares it.
2. **The unusable replies do not damage the contrast as an artefact; no prompt, follow-up or model change is chosen.** 40 of 51 final failures are prose or unseen-file replies that follow the gold being absent (final unusable with a gold unit in context: BM25 3.7%, RRF 1.8%; without: 45.3%, 41.2%); strict format faults are 20 of 400 first replies and 3 of 400 finally. Candidates: (a) a prompt format change is not supported (format faults are 0.75% of units after follow-ups); (b) a different follow-up policy: the two re-asks rescue 43% of unusable units at 29% of the calls, mostly where the gold is present (73% against 28%); a third re-ask or a different message has no data behind it; (c) a different model: no cross-model data. Residual unexplained: 8 final failures with a gold unit shown and 8 empty replies after a re-ask: too few for a test; no test is proposed. If the engineer wants the cheapest test of whether declines are avoidable, it is one more run of the 100 dev instances' 90 first-unusable units with a changed re-ask message (about 90 to 180 calls, one day), a proposal only.
3. **Unusable replies count as failures in the resolved rate, identically for all arms** (a unit with no usable edit is unresolved; the same follow-up policy, parser and counting for every arm). In a pilot-style comparison this puts the usability gap (8.5 points [1.0, 16.5] after follow-ups) inside the BM25-to-RRF difference, which is right: the gap is mostly the first stage not showing the code (analysis 3). A resolved rate among usable replies is reported beside it, labelled as conditional on a post-first-stage outcome.

**Not known.** Resolved rate and its discordance (nothing graded; dev instances have no executable environment); whether SEARCH blocks that match only after whitespace squashing (28) or are absent from the excerpts (78 blocks, 64 units) apply to the repository (the rule of application is Q69); whether the executable-subset rates match the extrapolation; whether another model, prompt or budget behaves alike; why empty replies follow a re-ask; any comparison with trained-checkpoint arms (none exist as contexts).

**Dead ends.** None in the analysis itself; one decision was reversed before the numbers were final: the first draft of the repository table split Pro instance ids by their hash suffix and was corrected to the `owner__name` form (the corrected table is the stored one).

## E-RQ3-PATCHAPPLY: turning edit blocks into the executor's patch; apply rates of the pilot's stored edits; end-to-end check on gold-derived edits. Exploratory (apply rates) and apparatus validation, 2026-11-08 (decision D110)

Status: **exploratory for the apply rates (100 development instances, outside the pre-registered RQ tables); apparatus validation for the end-to-end check.** No model was called and no RQ3 run was made. Evidence class: apply rates measured on 100 dev instances (349 usable units, 493 blocks); pipeline check measured on gold-derived edits of 20 executable instances. Code: `ril/agent/providers/patch_convert.py`, `ril/evaluation/gold_edits.py`, `ril/evaluation/patch_validation.py`, `ril/analysis/rq3_patch_apply.py`, `scripts/rq3_patch.py`, jobs `scripts/ada/rq3_patch/`; tests `tests/unit/test_patch_convert.py`, `test_gold_edits.py`, `test_patch_validation.py`, `test_rq3_patch_apply.py`. Outputs, commands, input hashes: `results/rq3_patch/` (README there).

**Decision D110** (reasoned; fixed before any model output is graded; same for every arm).
1. **Exact match is primary.** A block's SEARCH text must occur in its file as an exact string and exactly once (overlapping occurrences count); a file that does not exist at the base commit fails the block. An instance's edit set is atomic: any failing block means no patch, the instance is unresolved (no partial credit, no repair).
2. **Whitespace-tolerant is a labelled sensitivity**: line endings and trailing whitespace of every line normalised, blank lines at the start and end of the SEARCH text ignored, still exactly one match, replacement inserted as given; no fuzzy matching, no indentation repair, no edit-distance search. Reported beside the exact result wherever the resolved rate is.
3. One switch in the converter (`exact` or `tolerant`); never chosen per arm, instance or after results.
4. An unusable reply stays unresolved (D109); a block that fails to apply is an apply failure, reported separately, also unresolved.
5. **Tactical gap-fill (recorded, not a change of the rule):** blocks of one file are applied in the order of the reply, each to the file as the earlier blocks left it, so the failure classes stay the four of the decision (`file_missing`, `no_match`, `multiple_matches`, `parse`). A repeated block therefore fails (`no_match`), and a block may match text an earlier block produced. A first version matched every block against the base text and added an `overlap` class; it was dropped because it was stricter than the decision and needed a fifth class. Its effect is reported below as a sensitivity. Where a block's replacement is inserted into a file with CRLF line endings the inserted lines carry LF as given.

### 1. Apply rates of the pilot's stored edits (measurement 1; `rq3_patch_apply_*.csv`)

Inputs: the 349 usable units (final reply usable by the pilot's parser; 400 units = 100 dev instances x two first stages x two runs; call log and items hashes as in D109). Base-commit text of the 120 files the 349 units name was read with `git cat-file` from the repository caches of account A at the base commit recorded in the instance caches (job 64286, 120 of 120 present, 0 undecodable; no checkout, no clone). Nothing graded.

| first stage | units | usable | applied, non-empty diff | applied, empty diff (SEARCH equals REPLACE) | file_missing | no_match | multiple_matches | usable and applying (share of all units, Wilson interval) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM25, exact | 200 | 166 | 122 | 7 | 0 | 37 | 0 | 129 (64.5% [57.7, 70.8]) |
| RRF, exact | 200 | 183 | 147 | 0 | 0 | 33 | 3 | 147 (73.5% [67.0, 79.1]) |
| both, exact | 400 | 349 | 269 | 7 | 0 | 70 | 3 | 276 (69.0% [64.3, 73.3]) |
| BM25, tolerant | 200 | 166 | 122 | 7 | 0 | 37 | 0 | 129 (64.5%) |
| RRF, tolerant | 200 | 183 | 147 | 0 | 0 | 33 | 3 | 147 (73.5%) |
| both, tolerant | 400 | 349 | 269 | 7 | 0 | 70 | 3 | 276 (69.0%) |

- **The tolerant reading rescues none of the 73 exact-failing units** (0 of 37 BM25, 0 of 36 RRF) and fails no unit that applies exactly. Whitespace is not what makes blocks fail here; D109's 28 blocks that match only after squashing all whitespace differ in indentation or line layout, which the tolerant reading deliberately does not repair.
- **Failing blocks, each on its own (493 blocks):** 396 apply with a change and 11 with none, 3 are `multiple_matches` (all RRF, text shown in the excerpts), 63 are `no_match` and quote text found nowhere in the named file (all 63 are also not in the excerpts shown), none is `file_missing`. Blocks whose text is in the file but not in the excerpts shown: 20, and all 20 apply exactly (the model recalled real code); failing blocks quoting text that is in the file but not in the excerpts: 0. So the 18% of usable units with a block outside the excerpts (D109) split into units that apply anyway and units whose block is invented text; the second group is the apply failures.
- **Effect on the usable counts.** Usable only, 166 (BM25) against 183 (RRF) of 200; usable and applying, 129 against 147: the gap between arms goes from 17 to 18 units (8.5 to 9.0 points of the units), the shares from 83.0 / 91.5% to 64.5 / 73.5%. Applying units are 78% of usable ones for BM25 (129 of 166) and 80% for RRF (147 of 183). Of the 276 applying units, 7 have an empty diff and cannot resolve anything (they leave the base code as it is).
- **Sensitivity to the in-order rule** (`rq3_patch_apply_rescue.csv`): under a stricter base-text rule (no two blocks of a file may overlap in the base text) 5 of the 276 applying units (BM25 2, RRF 3) would fail instead.
- Multi-block replies are common: 252 units have one block, 97 have two to six.

### 2. End-to-end check on gold-derived edits (measurement 2; `validation_gold_derived.csv`, `validation_draw.json`)

Draw: seed 0, 20 of the 377 executable instances, one instance for each of the six repositories with at least 5 executable instances (django, sympy, pytest, xarray, astropy, sphinx) chosen uniformly within the repository, the other 14 places uniformly from the remaining 371 (so by size); requests is drawn by size, pylint, seaborn and flask not at all; the draw was made once, not repeated. Result: django 9, xarray 3, sympy 3, pytest 2, astropy 1, requests 1, sphinx 1.

Derivation (`ril/evaluation/gold_edits.py`): one block per hunk of the gold patch; SEARCH is the hunk's base-side lines (the patch's context lines and the removed lines), REPLACE its result-side lines; if the SEARCH text is not unique in the file one line of context is added on each side until it is; creation, deletion and rename of a file, or a hunk that does not sit at the base text, is a derivation limit, reported, not worked around. The blocks are written in the pilot's reply format, read back with the pilot's own parser and converted with the exact reading, so the path tested is the path of a reply. Each converted patch was first compared with the gold patch by tree id (apply both to the base commit in a temporary index) and then applied by the executor at the base commit, tests run as in E-RQ3-EXEC, one run each.

| instance | hunks = blocks | converted | tree equals gold | executor | FAIL_TO_PASS | PASS_TO_PASS | executor seconds (gold-run mean) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| astropy__astropy-14598 | 2 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 175 of 175 | 175 (240) |
| django__django-11734 | 3 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 275 of 275 | 27 |
| django__django-12308 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 2 of 2 | 20 of 20 | 11 |
| django__django-13568 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 13 of 13 | 8 |
| django__django-13569 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 66 of 66 | 8 |
| django__django-14011 | 3 | yes | yes | resolved | 17 of 17 | 2 of 2 | 78 |
| django__django-14404 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 2 of 2 | 323 of 323 | 55 |
| django__django-14725 | 5 | yes | yes | resolved | 3 of 3 | 64 of 64 | 148 |
| django__django-15503 | 6 | yes | yes | resolved | 2 of 2 | 78 of 78 | 111 |
| django__django-16136 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 2 of 2 | 7 of 7 | 13 |
| psf__requests-1142 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 5 of 5 | 60 |
| pydata__xarray-2905 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved (rerun, job 64304) | 1 of 1 | 364 of 364 | 345 |
| pydata__xarray-4966 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved (rerun, job 64304) | 4 of 4 | 21 of 21 | 323 |
| pydata__xarray-6721 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 1,405 of 1,405 | 972 |
| pytest-dev__pytest-10081 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 63 of 63 | 14 |
| pytest-dev__pytest-5631 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 15 of 15 | 15 |
| sphinx-doc__sphinx-7910 | 2 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 16 of 16 | 139 |
| sympy__sympy-12096 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 43 of 43 | 20 |
| sympy__sympy-14531 | 11 | yes | yes | resolved | 2 of 2 | 84 of 84 | 13 |
| sympy__sympy-23413 | 1 | yes | yes | resolved | 1 of 1 | 2 of 2 | 12 |

**20 of 20 converted, 20 of 20 trees identical to the gold patch's, 20 of 20 resolved** (FAIL_TO_PASS and PASS_TO_PASS all pass, as with the gold patch). No converter bug and no derivation limit appeared in this draw. The first execution of two xarray instances (2905, 4966) failed at environment creation after 6 s with a name-resolution error from the node (the package index could not be reached); that is infrastructure, not the patch: the same two converted patches were run again unchanged (job 64304) and resolved. Time column: executor wall time for the one run; the gold runs of E-RQ3-EXEC are in `results/rq3_exec/` (mean of two runs shown where the instance is slower than 100 s). Conversion itself takes about 0.2 s per instance; the conversion times in the derive job include a bare clone for the first instance of a repository and are not a conversion cost.

**What this does and does not show.** The pipeline (reply text, parser, converter, executor) reproduces the gold result on 20 instances, so a model reply that applies is graded the way the gold patch is. Gold-derived blocks are anchored by the patch's own context lines, which a model's blocks need not be; the 63 invented-text blocks of section 1 are exactly the case this cannot cover. Only exact reading was run through the executor (the tolerant reading is a different patch only when a block differs from the file in whitespace, which the pilot's data did not show).

### 3. Cost against expectation (measurement 3; `results/logs/ada_jobs.csv`, account A)

Expected before running: under 0.5 CPU-hours (20 instances, mean 38 s per run of E-RQ3-EXEC, peak memory under 450 MB at p99). Measured: validation job 64288 24.5 min wall, 4 allocated CPUs (1.6 allocated CPU-hours), rerun 64304 5.8 min (0.4), a cancelled first validation start 64287 1.7 min (0.1; cancelled because it had started with the first, stricter converter, see decision item 5), snapshot jobs 64285 and 64286 4 s each (64285 discarded: it derived the repository name of Pro-style instance ids wrongly and reported 59 of 120 files absent; the reader now fails when a repository or commit cannot be read, so a missing mirror cannot look like a missing file, and the mirror is found by name prefix). Total about 2.1 allocated CPU-hours, 4 jobs of use; the executor's own time summed over the 22 runs is 0.71 hours of 2 workers. The excess over the expectation is the bare clones (django, sympy, astropy: a few minutes), one slow instance (xarray-6721, 972 s, 1,405 PASS_TO_PASS tests) and the rerun. Local work: apply tables on the laptop, seconds. No GPU.

**Not known.** Resolved rate of any model patch (nothing graded; the dev instances have no executable environment); how the apply rates carry to the 377 executable instances (these are dev instances with an easier context mix, D109 section 5, and one model at one temperature); whether the invented-text blocks (63) would be repaired by any tolerant rule (the tolerant rule rescues none, a fuzzy rule was excluded by the decision and not tried); the tolerant reading through the executor (not run); how blocks written by a different model apply. Replacement text in CRLF files is untested against real instances (none of the 120 named files was checked for line endings in this entry).

**Remains before the first RQ3 model run.** (1) The model and scaffold decision (ADR-007, ADR-008) and Q45. (2) Contexts of trained checkpoints (blocked on the RQ2 final runs); the declared fallback is RAW against the untrained encoder. (3) A driver that, per unit, parses the reply, reads the 377 instances' base-commit files, converts (both readings from one stored reply set) and passes the patch to the executor; the pieces exist, the driver does not. (4) Prompts for the 377 executable instances (the pilot prepared 100 dev prompts). (5) The 28 sphinx test-start failures are outside the subset. (6) The cluster node-local DNS outage seen here argues for the executor's retry on a failed environment step (a re-run of the unit is the current remedy).

**Dead ends.** A first converter matched blocks against the base text and had an `overlap` class; it was replaced (decision item 5). A first snapshot job guessed repository names from the id and gave a false 59 absent files; fixed and rerun.

## E-RQ3-DRIVER: held-out model-check slice, prompts of the 377, the driver, analysis plan, power with run noise, cost model. Apparatus and plan, 2026-11-08 (decisions D111 to D114)

Status: **apparatus and plan; no model was called, no resolved rate exists.** Evidence class: reasoned for the decisions; measured for the replay test (30 of 30), the prompt counts and the screen findings; estimated (simulation) for power; tokens measured in the pilot and prices unverified for the cost. Code: `ril/agent/providers/{driver,holdout,spend,model_choice,rq3_prompts,replay_variants,cluster}.py` (and `run_unit` split out of `pilot_run.run_units`, `thinking_config` in `GeminiConfig`), `ril/analysis/{rq3_results,rq3_power_noise,rq3_cost}.py`, `scripts/rq3_driver.py`, jobs `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/` (`prepare`, `replay`, `model_check`), `configs/agent/prices.yaml`, `gemini_limits_paid.template.yaml`; tests `tests/unit/test_rq3_{holdout,driver,prompts_and_variants,results,driver_cli}.py`. Outputs and the full text of D111 to D114: `results/rq3_driver/`, `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md` ("Driver, held-out slice and analysis plan"). Jobs (account A, CPU only, `results/logs/ada_jobs.csv`, session RQ3D): 64589 (first prompt pass), 64597 (screen findings), 64598 (prompts), 64590 (replay).

**Why.** The free tier costs about two calendar days per arm-run (533 calls against 450 a day); the pilot left 276 of 400 units applying and 63 failing blocks quoting text found nowhere in the file, so a weak model may leave the resolved rate near zero, which no arm comparison can detect. The project needed the whole path as one tested driver, a set of executable instances kept back to choose the model on graded results, a fixed analysis plan that accounts for run noise, and a spend guard.

**Decisions.** D111 held-out slice (50 of 377, seed 0, stratified; django 30, sympy 10, astropy 2, pytest 2, sphinx 2, xarray 2, pylint 1, requests 1); D112 model-choice rule (cheapest candidate with a resolved rate of at least 15% on the slice, else the strongest with the floor reported as not met); D113 scaffold and analysis plan (ADR-007 Accepted `provisional`, Q45 answered: one primary contrast, RAW against the untrained encoder, paired bootstrap and sign-flip permutation on the 327, k = 3 if the spend allows else 2); D114 spend guard (cap required, worst-case check before each call). Full text in `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md`.

**Results.**
1. *Prompts.* 887 of 1,131 pass the screen, 244 rejected (135 address, 101 home path, 8 both); 109 instances have a rejected prompt, 57 have all three rejected; complete-case counts 232 of 327 confirmatory and 36 of 50 held-out; RRF sendable 258 and 39. 228 distinct unreviewed addresses or path strings; 111 of the 195 addresses look like a real address, 69 are placeholder domains, 14 are GitHub no-reply addresses (a mechanical suggestion, not a review). Text before the excerpts byte-identical across arms (320 of 320 with a prompt). Issue text from the table for all 377, equal to the folder text for 320 of 320.
2. *Replay (job 64590).* 20 of 20 gold-derived edit sets `resolved`; 4 of 4 altered SEARCH texts `apply_failed:no_match`, 3 of 3 ambiguous ones `apply_failed:multiple_matches`, 3 of 3 import-line `raise` variants `executed_unresolved`. 11 min 50 s on 4 allocated CPUs.
3. *Power with run noise.* Minimum detectable difference at power 0.8: 4.4 to 9.7 points at N = 327 across k = 1 to 3 and the scenarios (5.5 to 9.7 at k = 1, 4.4 to 7.7 at k = 3); 5.4 to over 10 at N = 232; size of the test 2.4 to 5.0%. Which inputs are assumptions: `rq3_power_run_noise_assumptions.json`.
4. *Cost (estimated).* One arm-run of 327: about 2.0 dollars on the cheapest candidate (3.8 high), 8.9 (15.7) on a Flash-class model at the stored unverified prices; the 50 held-out about 0.31 and 1.4; 3 arms x k = 3 about 18 and 80. Recommended cap for the graded check of the two priced candidates: 4 dollars in all (1 and 3 per run), provider hard cap 10 to leave room for a third candidate and a rerun.

**Dead ends.** (a) The first prompt pass (job 64589) took issue text from the query folders only and skipped the 57 instances without an L2 label (no folder text); the table's `problem_statement` is used for all 377 (equal to the folder text for the 320 that have both). (b) The pilot's `prepare_items` raises on the first screen finding; a whole run could not be prepared, so a variant that reports rejections was added. (c) An environment variable carrying the operator's address into the job was refused by the harness and is not used; the screen rejects any address not on the reviewed list, so the operator's address is rejected anyway.

**Not known.** Any resolved rate; whether the screen will be relaxed (Q70) and therefore the confirmatory N; the model; the price of a third candidate; whether a stronger model bills thought tokens (the pilot model billed none).

**Remains before the first confirmatory run.** The graded model check (engineer: billing, hard cap, key on the cluster, billed-tier limits, price check, Q70), the choice of k, trained-checkpoint contexts (blocked on RQ2), the 16,000-token prompts (optional, not run).

## E-LIT-SCAFFOLD: the five agent-scaffold papers read in full, scaffold compared with how they use it. Literature, 2026-11-08 (decision D116)

Status: **done; no model call, no cluster job.** Evidence class: read in full. Answers Q63.

**Why.** The standing rule makes a paper a source only after a full, versioned, checked reading. ADR-007 and the report rest on retrieval-supplied one-shot repair (edit blocks as in Agentless); the scaffold table of `docs/EVAL_API.md` names five papers.

**Papers (PDF fetched from the paper's own arXiv page, 2026-11-08).** Agentless v2 (25 pp), SWE-agent v3 (118 pp), OpenHands v3 (38 pp): new notes, `scripts/check_paper_note.py` PASS (page counts equal `pdfinfo`, 57, 69 and 19 numbers found on the stated pages). LocAgent v2 (18 pp) and CoSIL v3 (17 pp): notes from 2026-10-17 re-verified (PASS on freshly fetched PDFs, 56 and 69 numbers) and extended with a scaffold section from re-read passages, not a second full reading. None is unread.

**Findings.** (1) The scaffold shares the edit format and the retrieval/repair separation with Agentless; it has no sampling, no test selection and no follow-ups in the paper, and a different context shape (10 lines around locations against 8,000 tokens of ranked units). (2) SWE-agent and OpenHands cannot isolate the retriever (shell open); costs about 1.6 to 1.7 dollars per Lite instance. (3) Fixed-repair swaps in LocAgent and CoSIL give small resolved-rate gains (+1.6 Pass@1 for +15 Acc@5; 14 instances of 300 across four localizers against a ten-instance reproduction spread). (4) SWE-agent six runs: mean 17.94%, pass@6 32.67%, so at least 15.3 points of instances flip between runs (inference). (5) Contradictions with project text and between papers: listed in the ADR-007 addendum and each note.

**Dead ends / limits.** SWE-agent's trajectory pages (45 to 116) were read without the numbered source listings inside observations; the note carries the caveat and the header can be downgraded if the rule requires them. Agentless v1 and OpenHands/SWE-agent earlier versions were not read, so the difference between Agentless's 27.3 on Lite in OpenHands and its 32.00% in v2 is unexplained.

**Report slots fed** (`reports/final/final_report.tex`, not edited here): Related work, a new paragraph on agent scaffolds and the repair stage; RQ3 Design paragraph (scaffold wording, DEV-037); the Threats todo (scaffold sensitivity, model non-determinism).


**Addendum 2026-11-08 (decision D121): the lead's rulings on the reading, applied.** No model call, no cluster job. Evidence class: read in full (per the notes) for the facts; reasoned for the rulings; the code change is tested on synthetic rows.
(1) *Note header.* The SWE-agent note's header now reads `read: in full except the numbered source-code listings inside the appendix trajectories (pp. 45 to 116); every agent turn, error message, patch and summary was read`, and its depth caveat is a statement of scope. `scripts/check_paper_note.py` (`ril/util/paper_note.py`) accepts that qualified form and fails a `read: full` header above a note that describes a skipped part, a skipped range outside the document, a page-count mismatch, and a number cited from a skipped page (6 new tests in `tests/unit/test_paper_note.py`, 20 pass); a test over the tracked notes finds no other note that skipped part of its own document.
(2) *Name.* "Agentless-style" is replaced by "retrieval-supplied one-shot repair" in `docs/RQ3_PILOT.md`, ADR-007 (new addendum, no overwrite of the old ones beyond the phrase), this entry, and the Agentless note; no code, config or docstring used the phrase; the report did not use it and now names the scaffold. Ids and paths are unchanged; the retired name is a glossary entry of the project story.
(3) *Any-of-k.* `ril/analysis/rq3_results.py`: columns `any_of_k_share` (with interval) per arm and `any_of_k_difference` (with interval) per contrast, both readings, status `secondary_exploratory`, no test; the primary contrast is unchanged (4 new tests on synthetic rows). The RQ2 analysis shares no code with it, so its scope is unchanged.
(4) *Unexplained difference.* OpenHands lists Agentless at 27.3 on Lite, Agentless v2 reports 32.00%: recorded as unexplained in both notes, in this entry and in the ADR; no reason offered.
(5) *Report.* `reports/final/final_report.tex`: related-work paragraph on agent scaffolds and the repair stage, scaffold naming and scope in the RQ3 design, the secondary reading in the analysis plan, a scaffold-sensitivity paragraph and a model non-determinism paragraph in the threats, four bibliography entries; one sentence of the related-work introduction now states the SWE-agent exception; each claim carries its note's page reference in a source comment; `scripts/check_report.py`: 0 findings, 24 open slots (unchanged; the threats slot was narrowed to the remaining part).
(6) *For workstream C.* A local note lists what `docs/EVAL_API.md` asks that the papers do not describe and asks C to update that file; it was not edited here.
**Dead ends / limits.** The checker was re-run on freshly fetched PDFs of the three notes changed here: SWE-agent v3 (118 pages, the qualified header accepted, 69 numbers found, none on pp. 45 to 116), OpenHands v3 (38 pages, 19 numbers) and Agentless v2 (25 pages, 57 numbers): PASS. Earlier versions of Agentless were still not read, so the 27.3 against 32.00% difference stays unexplained.

## E-RQ2-READINESS: which stages between the mined store and the final RQ2 tables are missing, broken or unmeasured at full scale? Audit, dry run and plan, 2026-11-08 (decision D115)

Status: **audit and plan-only tooling; nothing trained, no GPU job, no model call; the running mining job was read, never changed.** Evidence class: measured (dry run, scheduler accounting, the running store's state log), projected (linear in a measured rate), reasoned (the rest). Product: `docs/RQ2_LAUNCH_PLAN.md` (chain, data-location options with calendar days, job graph, preflight list, gaps, decisions, next work packages). Outputs: `results/rq2_readiness/` (dry-run logs of job 64656, the evaluation-set check of job 64658, the partial yield, queue-wait summary). Code: `ril/training/config_io.py` (configuration loading and hashing moved out of the torch module, names re-exported, hash unchanged: six stored runs reproduce their directory prefix), `ril/training/rq2_plan.py` and `scripts/rq2_plan.py` (cell table, in-memory rendering and checks, budget on two bases, job graph, fresh-noise and distinct-example checks), `ril/evaluation/eval_plan.py` (store and cost check of evaluation sets), `ril/data_mining/state_yield.py` and `mine_pilot.py yield`, a one-line fix in `ril/data_mining/report.py`, `scripts/ada/rq2_readiness.sbatch`; tests `test_rq2_plan.py` (13), `test_eval_plan.py` (3), `test_state_yield.py` (2), `test_report_stored_summary.py` (1).

**Decision D115 (tactical, no design content).** (1) The plan uses the seeds 100, 101, 102 and refuses injected cells on seeds 0 to 2, because a noise draw is keyed on the run's seed (`noise.inject`) and D82 requires fresh draws; the count of three is O7. (2) Unit costs are kept as named constants with their evidence class, and the budget is shown on both the protocol basis (reasoned, reproduces 38.9) and the measured basis (28.7). (3) The mining yield is read from `state.jsonl` so that it works for a running or fault-stopped job. No cell, arm, teacher, metric or claim was changed; the choices that are the engineer's are listed in the plan, section 7.

**Dry run (job 64656, account A, CPU, 1 min 15 s, node-local scratch, 20 directories of the pilot store).** Materialise 13 s (921 MB peak), rank-prepare 7 s (18 `non_test` directories), BM25 ranks 6 s, feature table 1 s, held-out selection 1 s, cache 4 s, FILTER size 1 s (15 of 18 examples, 70 of 110 positives), overlap audit 24 s (0 dropped, 0 flagged at floor 20), plan-only launcher 0 s (36 runs, 36 distinct hashes), evaluation-set check 17 s. Every step returned 0 on current main. Not run: the report step on the pilot (the run root with its summary was removed after the store copy was verified; the tracked pilot summary renders after the fix).

**Measured.** Evaluation sets (job 64658): the eight repositories 69,961 distinct unit texts (equal to the build's number) and 224 queries; dev with ansible 212,834 texts, 680 queries; the six Verified folders with stores 114,503 texts, 734 queries; six Verified folders have no stores. Queue waits (scheduler accounting, 2026-10-20 to 2026-11-08, jobs over a minute): account A GPU 44 jobs, median 0.03 h, p90 2.75 h, maximum 8.25 h; account C one GPU job. Running mining job at 2,985 state rows (12:12 IST): 771 rows in 67.5 min since the relaunch; of 2,835 rows 62.6% mined, 1.2% quarantined; store 1.1 GB, projected 3.7 GB at 10,000.

**Findings.** The launch cannot start from what exists: no final cell configurations or run script (gap 1), one run cannot score three evaluation sets (2), no full-scale prep script or drop-list step (3), and the mined cache (5.6 GB projected) does not fit account A's 5.1 GB free (4). Unmeasured and large: a dense teacher over the mined store (upper bound 21 GPU-hours, gap 7). The 1,000-update sensitivity cannot reach the matched unique-example count on any mined store (gap 10). Six of twelve Verified folders have no stores (gap 8). The protocol's eight-repository cost (0.48 per checkpoint, reasoned) is 0.178 measured, so the measured-basis total is 28.7 of 46.7.

**Dead ends and surprises.** (a) The first evaluation-set check counted lines of the store shards (233,064 for the eight) instead of distinct texts (69,961); fixed and tested. (b) The login node's interpreter has no numpy, so the check runs in a job. (c) `report` could not be run on the pilot store at all (no summary file after the store conversion), which is why `yield` exists. (d) Writing the plan graph showed that injected-noise jobs need nothing from mining, which shortens the critical path by 11 hours in the split option.

**Not known.** The `non_test` and `pool_aligned` counts of the full store, the dense teacher's de-duplicated cost, the Verified look's real cost, whether account C's owner agrees to GPU work on it (the operating notes and the state page disagree on who owns it), memory at full scale beyond the projected 26 GB.

**Torch tests after the move (job 64669, account A, 23 s, no GPU).** The cluster's torch-marked set (hooks identity, retention in training, noise arms, loss, measurement, end-to-end training, run-batch, curve hook, training reproducibility, leakage) passes on the commit with the loader moved out of the training module: 41 passed. The local run (`make test` equivalent, no torch): 1,366 passed, 5 skipped.

**Addendum 2026-11-09 (decision D122, tactical: reconciliation of two sessions that ran this task, no design content).** (1) The mining jobs ended: 10,000 state rows (mined 6,230, no Python change 2,607, no positive 1,040, quarantined 123), store 1.8 GB, no `HALT` (read only); the 3.7 GB projection above was too high. (2) The FILTER pool is projected at about 3,430 directories (6,230 x 0.9245 `non_test` x 0.595 kept, both shares from the pilot) against 3,200 visits of a 100-update run, a margin of about 7% before drops; a protocol rule for a pool smaller than the visits is needed (options (a) to (c), recommendation (a), not chosen; `docs/RQ2_LAUNCH_PLAN.md` 9.2). An example is a directory (`data_loader.py`); the protocol's "pairs" is ambiguous. (3) Account C lacks the eight repositories (480 MB on A); the dev prep, e3 cache and the injected-noise pool and score files are equal on A and C (size manifests for two directories, sha256 for five files). (4) A has 5.1 GB free, the mined cache is projected at 5.6 GB, C has 26 GB free. (5) Critical path with `--family-accounts mined=C injected=A`: 24.0 h (1.0 day) at the median wait, 53.9 h (2.2 days) at p90, against 34.9 h and 72.9 h on one account; 28.7 GPU-hours on the measured basis of the 46.7 cap. (6) Retention options (i) to (iii) against the test role of the eight (9.4). (7) No Holm or verdict code exists (the one hit is a job label); scope in 9.5. (8) Account B has no SSH alias. Evidence classes: measured (counts, disk, hashes, plan-tool output), projected (pool size, cache size), reasoned (rule options). No GPU job, no change to the mining store.

## E-REPORT-DRAFT-1: the settled sections of the final report, drafted from stored evidence. Writing, 2026-11-08

Status: done. No cluster job, no model call, no GPU. Evidence class: nothing new is measured; every number of the prose is read from a stored file or an entry named in a comment beside it.

**What was written** (`reports/final/final_report.tex`, 13 pages two-column): Introduction (claim and non-claims, contributions), Background (retrieval for agents, evaluation levels, resolved rate), Related work (corrected sentence on the training corpus; the corpus paper's note says CodeRankEmbed was trained on it, not that the published pool builds on it), Data and ground truth (published pool and its limitation, held-out list and leakage controls, mined set and its two readings, overlap audit, label audit design, contamination), System with stated limits, Evaluation setup, RQ2 protocol with arms and noise design, RQ3 method (executable subset and skew, scaffold, edit rules, model-call pilot, analysis plan, power), Threats (T12 to T18 added), Reproducibility. Results appear only as the existing exploratory tables plus three new generated ones (`contamination`, `rq3_subset`, `rq3_power`, built by `ril/analysis/report_tables.py` from `results/`).

**Checks.** `scripts/check_report.py` (`ril/util/report_check.py`, tests `tests/unit/test_report_check.py`): every prose number is closed by a source comment, citations and references resolve, open slots are listed. Compiled with `tectonic`: 13 pages, no error, no undefined reference, no overfull box; underfull boxes come from float placement. The course's report limit is not in the repository; sections were kept tight and the page count is stated, not checked against a limit.

**Conflicts in the sources, later correction followed.** (1) The skeleton and the protocol say the noise-audit sample is stratified under each teacher; the built sheet is stratified by BM25 only (D99). (2) The skeleton says RQ3 uses McNemar over four retrievers and placeholder discordances; D113 fixes one primary contrast (RAW against the untrained encoder), paired bootstrap and sign-flip, and the run-noise power table. (3) The skeleton says the scaffold and model are open; the scaffold is accepted provisionally (ADR-007, D113). (4) The earlier count of 263 buildable instances is superseded by 377 executable (D106). (5) The 1.22 token ratio is against an embedding model's tokenizer; the agent model's own counts gave 1.00.

**Open slots (24) and what each waits for.** Abstract, contributions on contrasts, conclusion: E-RQ2-FILTER, E-RQ3-AGENT. Full mined set size, yield, surviving counts after the audit: E-MINE-FULL, E-OVERLAP-AUDIT. Audited noise rate and agreement: E-LABEL-AUDIT-SHEET. Edge recovery: E-GRAPH-BUILD. Latency table: E-LAT-STAGES. RQ1 ladder reading: E-RQ1-*. Stacked expansion: E-GRAPH-L3. Sibling noise: E-PILOT-NOISE-SIB. Confirmatory contrasts and exploratory arms: E-RQ2-FILTER, E-RQ2-RAW, E-RQ2-CTRL-SUBRAW, E-RQ2-WEIGHT, E-RQ2-CURRICULUM, E-RQ2-SEC-QQ. Evaluation of the eight repositories: E-EVAL-REPOS-BUILD. Agent runs, power from graded runs, agent threats, token budget: E-RQ3-AGENT, E-POWER. A missing citation for the benchmark's verified subset (no note exists); the related-work refresh.

**Not written for lack of evidence.** Any confirmatory contrast, resolved rate, noise rate, full-mined-set figures, RQ1 ladder, latency table, edge-recovery table.

**Dead ends.** None.

## E-A1-TAKEOVER: reproduction of the A1-DEV cells with stored query rows, and a BM25 variant inside RRF(BM25, dense). Measured (dev only, exploratory), 2026-11-08 (decisions D117 to D119)

Status: **done.** Done by the lead while the owner of workstream A was away, on a separate branch from the owner's tip (the owner's commits are untouched). Evidence class: measured on the development folders at chunk level; exploratory; the Verified folders were not read. Configurations: `configs/experiments/a_bm25_dev.yaml` (hash 245d4655aa5f, as logged by the owner) and `configs/experiments/a_bm25_fused.yaml` (hash 653973eea23a, rule fixed before the run).

**Decisions (the lead's rulings on the owner's requests; D115 and D116 were taken by other work in flight, so these start at D117).** D117 (Q57): `RESOLVER_VERSION` becomes v0.1 at the next cache rebuild; caches keyed on the resolver string rebuild once; no rebuild is forced now (evidence: measured in A2a, 2 of 4,163 requests edges change, the figure has no stored diff; cheapest flip: none needed). D118 (Q58): the incremental updater is the per-commit graph build for the agent setting and for freshness measurements (100 of 100 updates equal to the full rebuild, minimum speedup 1.05, never slower); the full build stays the default of `build_repository` and of every frozen artefact until the agent setting needs the per-commit path (evidence: measured). D119 (Q59): the variants of A1-PILOT and k1/b are run one factor at a time on the development repositories with the E1 dev sweep default as the regression control; the pilot gain is an upper bound (docstrings share the function's vocabulary), the dev run decides (evidence: measured locally, exploratory). Of D119 the dev run covers the tokenizer variants and k1/b at chunk level; the field-weight variant and unit-level granularity need unit texts at each base commit and are not run (see "Cut or not done").

**(a) Reproduction.** Jobs 64665 (account A, CPU, 4 allocated CPUs, node gnode004, Python 3.10.12, 14 min 56 s; run files in node-local scratch, copied at the end, nothing deleted) re-ran stage 1 and stage 2 with the owner's code, configuration and the same dev folders and queries (data of the E1 dev sweep on account A; that it is the same data follows from the control and from all 228 values below being equal, ansible included). 162 run files, 30.6 MB, listed with size and SHA-256 in `results/logs/a1_bm25_dev_run_files_2026-11-08.json`; the files stay on account A. Query-level rows (cell, level, folder, query id, positives, nDCG@10, Recall@100) are `results/tables/a1_bm25_dev_query_rows.csv` (12,240 rows, 18 cells).

**Result.** Regression control: `ref` at k1 1.5 / b 0.75 on the five E1 dev folders gives 510 queries and pooled nDCG@10 23.8244, equal to the default row of the E1 dev sweep and to the owner's logged value. All 228 compared values (queries, nDCG@10 pooled and per level, Recall@100, differences and both intervals, for every cell of both stages) equal the logged ones (`results/tables/a1_bm25_dev_repro_compare.csv`, `results/logs/a1_bm25_dev_repro_2026-11-08.json`); the selection is the same: `split` at stage 1, k1 2.0 / b 0.75 at stage 2. Timings differ and are not compared (other node; `split` tokenising 2,989 chunks per second here, query p95 9.7 ms against the owner's 8.0).

**Reading (a).** The owner's A1-DEV statistics are reproducible from the stored configuration, code and data. The k1 2.0 cell is the best of 15 non-default cells of the k1 x b grid and sits on the edge of the grid; its clustered interval (+0.1, +1.0) was chosen after looking at the maximum, so it is optimistic; the candidate k1 2.0 stays **unconfirmed**, and the part inside the fused first stage is (b).

**(b) A BM25 variant inside the fusion.** Job 64671 (account A, CPU, node gnode006, 1 min 26 s): the default, `split` at k1 1.5 / b 0.75 (the tokenizer alone) and `split` at k1 2.0 / b 0.75 (the candidate), each alone and fused by RRF with the stored dense scores (no encoding): Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B at 512 tokens, k 20 (the dev-selected strongest first stage, primary) and CodeRankEmbed at 512 tokens, k 100 (the training base, D30). Filter-first reading as the chunk level of E-DENSE-FUSION, five dev folders, 355 queries at L2 (five repositories) and 155 at L3 (two repositories). Control check: the default cell reproduces the stored chunk ranks of E-DENSE-FUSION for BM25 and both fusions on 1,530 of 1,530 (query, system) pairs.

**Result.** nDCG@10 difference to the default inside the same system (points; query-level interval; repository-clustered interval; `results/tables/a1_bm25_fused.csv`):

| system | level | `split` k1 1.5 | `split` k1 2.0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| BM25 alone | L2 | +4.58 [+3.20, +6.02]; clustered [+1.79, +7.32] | +5.06 [+3.61, +6.57]; clustered [+2.27, +7.86] |
| RRF with Qwen3 (k 20) | L2 | +0.95 [-0.02, +1.89]; clustered [-0.62, +2.69] | +0.64 [-0.30, +1.55]; clustered [-0.97, +2.27] |
| RRF with CodeRankEmbed (k 100) | L2 | +1.04 [+0.04, +2.08]; clustered [-0.53, +2.84] | +1.13 [+0.18, +2.09]; clustered [-0.39, +2.71] |
| BM25 alone | L3 | +9.99 [+7.69, +12.39] | +10.78 [+8.41, +13.16] |
| RRF with Qwen3 (k 20) | L3 | +4.30 [+2.71, +6.05] | +4.20 [+2.55, +5.96] |
| RRF with CodeRankEmbed (k 100) | L3 | +5.67 [+3.87, +7.59] | +5.56 [+3.75, +7.37] |

Default means: BM25 19.26 (L2) and 34.29 (L3); RRF with Qwen3 28.67 and 47.39; RRF with CodeRankEmbed 23.18 and 41.47. L3 clustered intervals rest on two repositories and are not informative; the L3 reading is the query-level interval. Recall@100 at L3: `split` k1 1.5 +9.76 alone, +3.44 with Qwen3, +4.14 with CodeRankEmbed.

**Reading (b).** By the rule fixed before the run (a gain survives if the fused L2 difference has a repository-clustered interval above 0), **no BM25 variant's gain survives the fusion at L2**: the gain of about +4.6 to +5.1 alone falls to +0.6 to +1.1 inside RRF, and every fused L2 clustered interval includes 0 (two query-level intervals, both CodeRankEmbed cells, lie just above 0). At L3 the gain shrinks from about +10 to +4 to +6 and its query-level interval stays above 0. The candidate k1 2.0 adds nothing over the tokenizer once fused (L2 +0.64 against +0.95 with Qwen3, +1.13 against +1.04 with CodeRankEmbed; no interval computed for that pair). So identifier splitting is a gain for BM25 alone and a smaller, less certain one for the fused first stage the system would use; it is not yet an improvement of the system at L2. Limits: dev only, one seed, chunk level; zero-shot encoders; five repositories at L2 and two at L3; filter-first rather than the protocol reading (the control equals the E-DENSE-FUSION values by construction); the pooled difference with Qwen3 for `split` k1 1.5 is +1.97 with a clustered interval of [+0.19, +3.67], which is not the rule's test and is not used to overturn it. Cheapest flip: the same cell with the field-weight variant at unit level (needs unit texts), or on the Verified-six once, with the single final setting.

**Cut or not done.** The field-weight variant (`split_fields`) and unit-level granularity (unit texts needed); a larger k1 (the grid edge is not extended); an interval for k1 2.0 against `split` k1 1.5 inside the fusion; ansible is not in the fused cell (no chunk vectors for it); the owner's node-local timings are not reproduced (other node). Commands: `scripts/ada/a_bm25_dev_repro.sbatch` (job 64665), `scripts/ada/a_bm25_fused.sbatch` (job 64671), `python3 scripts/a_bm25_dev.py compare ...`.

**Dead ends.** None in the runs. The job logs landed in a directory named after the submit directory (`--output` is relative), as already noted in `docs/COMPUTE.md`.
## E-RQ3-PSEUDONYMISE: pseudonymise-and-restore for the RQ3 prompts; prompts rebuilt and re-screened; round trips on real data. Apparatus, 2026-11-08 (decision D120, closes Q70)

Status: **apparatus; no model was called, no resolved rate exists.** Evidence class: reasoned for the decision (the provider's terms read on 2026-11-08, effective 2026-03-23, and the counts of E-RQ3-DRIVER); measured for the counts and round trips below.

**Why.** The screen (written for the unpaid terms: submitted content may be used for the provider's products and read by reviewers, personal information must not be submitted; paid terms: not used to improve products, logged for a limited period to detect violations) rejected 244 of 1,131 prompts, mostly placeholder and maintainer addresses and user names in tracebacks quoted in issues. Dropping those instances would make the sample depend on whether an issue quotes a traceback or an address, which is not random with respect to difficulty. Pseudonymisation keeps the prompts clean under both tiers, so the tier does not change what is sent.

**Decision D120.** The same deterministic transform for every arm and instance, after assembly and before the screen: every e-mail address becomes `person<N>@example.invalid`, the user-name segment of every home-directory path (`/home/<name>`, `/home<k>/<name>`, `/Users/<name>`, `C:\Users\<name>`) becomes `user<N>`, N by first occurrence in the prompt, in the issue and in every excerpt (`ril/agent/providers/pseudonymise.py`). The mapping of each prompt is stored with it (`maps/<prompt hash>.json`, on the cluster, never printed, never tracked). The driver restores the reply before conversion (both readings), so an edit that quotes a placeholder matches the original file and writes the original text. The screen accepts exactly the two placeholder forms and nothing new; the reviewed list is unchanged; a pattern the screen still finds after the transform is a defect of the transform. Restore covers the whole reply (FILE lines too), which includes the SEARCH and REPLACE texts. Prompts sent in the free-tier pilot passed the screen as it was and are unaffected.

**Results (job 65086 prepare, 32 s; job 65087 checks, 9 min 44 s; account A, CPU, node-local scratch, nothing deleted).**
1. *Screen.* 1,131 of 1,131 prompts pass, 0 rejected, 0 skipped; 244 prompts touched (email only 135, path only 101, both 8, the same 244 the old screen rejected), by arm bm25 88, dense 76, rrf 80; 109 instances have a touched prompt. Restoring each prompt with its mapping gave back the original prompt byte for byte for all 1,131 (a mismatch would have been a rejection). Prefix before the excerpts identical across arms for 377 of 377 instances. Complete case: 377 of 377 instances, 327 confirmatory and 50 held-out, all with three sendable arms.
2. *Tokens (approximate counter of the assembly).* Per prompt the change is mean +0.72 (all) and +3.32 (touched), min -45, max +91; context alone mean +0.35, max +84. Contexts were assembled to at most 8,000: 14 of 1,131 exceed it after the transform, maximum overshoot 72 approximate tokens (0.9%), within the tolerance fixed before the run (1% of the budget, 80). The cost table of E-RQ3-DRIVER is unchanged (the effect is under 0.1% of a prompt).
3. *Excerpt round trip.* 1,126 excerpt lines carrying a placeholder (109 instances, 244 prompts): quoted as a model would, restored and converted against the base-commit file, 870 applied and wrote the original line, 256 matched more than once (the line is repeated in its file, a property of the file), 0 `no_match`.
4. *Gold-derived edits.* For the touched prompts, 241 of 244 give the gold tree after quote, restore and convert; 3 have no derived edits (derivation limits as in E-RQ3-PATCHAPPLY). **None of the gold-derived blocks contains a substituted string**, so this check is true by construction for them; the excerpt check above is the one that exercises the substitution.
5. *Replay on the real executor (same job, 10 touched instances, replies quoted in placeholder form and restored by the driver).* 20 of 20 outcome classes as expected: 10 of 10 gold replies `resolved`, 4 no-match, 3 multiple-match, 3 broken-code. None of the 10 instances has a substitution inside its gold-derived blocks (the draw prefers those that do; there were none).

**Dead ends.** (a) A first name pattern allowed `@`, so a path root directly followed by an address swallowed the placeholder and could not be restored; found by a fuzz test; names are now ASCII `A-Za-z0-9._-` ending in a letter, digit or underscore. (b) Two addresses with nothing between them, and a path root followed only by punctuation, are not invertible; the screen refuses them, none occurred in the 1,131 prompts.

**Not known.** Whether a model quotes placeholders faithfully (no model was called); a model that writes a new `/home/user<N>` path of its own gets it restored to a real name (rare, not measured). Whether the provider's data terms at the time of the graded run are those read on 2026-11-08.

**Remains.** The engineer's steps before the graded model check (billing with a hard cap, key, limits file, prices, models), listed in the hand-back of the session.

Files: `results/rq3_pseudonymise/` (prepare report, check report, per-line and gold tables, replay table), `scripts/ada/rq3_driver/pseudonym_check.sbatch`, tests `tests/unit/test_pseudonymise.py`, `test_pseudonym_check.py`.

## E-RQ2-ANALYSIS: the final RQ2 analysis as code (Holm, delta, verdicts, fallback, sealed order), written and tested before any final row exists. Apparatus, 2026-11-09 (decision D123)

Status: done; CPU only; no cluster job, no model call, no GPU. Evidence class: measured (simulations on synthetic rows with a known effect; reproduction of stored pilot summaries), reasoned (the narrowest readings where the protocol is silent). **Not run on final rows; no synthetic or pilot number enters a headline table** (the summary carries `source_kind` and the table builders refuse anything but `final`). Code: `ril/analysis/rq2_final.py` (analysis), `rq2_final_pilot.py` (stored pilot rows in the input form; pilot use only), `rq2_final_tables.py` (four LaTeX fragments through `scripts/report_tables.py`, built only when the summary of the final rows exists, `rq2_final_tables.SUMMARY_REL`), `scripts/rq2_analysis.py`; tests `tests/unit/test_rq2_final.py`, `test_rq2_final_pilot.py`, `test_rq2_final_tables.py`. Where each protocol rule lives in the code: `docs/RQ2_PROTOCOL.md` section 11 (last table).

**What it computes.** Input: one row per run, cell, seed, evaluation set, level, repository and query (`n_positives`, nDCG@10, Recall@100) and one diagnostics row per run (config hash, optimizer updates, examples seen, distinct directories seen, pool size). Output, in one hashed JSON summary: per cell and set the mean over queries with the repository-clustered interval (D17; query-level below three repositories, the other interval beside) and the per-seed means with their SD; the paired differences per seed and pooled over seeds; the two confirmatory contrasts of D69 at both levels, Holm within each family and set on the intervals (D72, adjusted intervals with the uncorrected ones beside, the adjusted p-value beside); the classification of every contrast against delta = 1.5 (D73) as reported effect, estimate with bounds or inconclusive; Recall@100 as the L3 metric with nDCG@10 beside it (D71); the section-6 claim per arm and final set (positive, level-specific, inconclusive, estimate with bounds) with the dev-sign check and the leave-largest-repository-out check of rule 3; the dose-response reading and the calibration sanity rule; the D80 fallback (whether the arms separate, hence the primary RQ3 contrast), stated from the rule; the distinct-directory diagnostics per arm, carried into every table; the hashes of both input files and the config hashes. The bootstrap is the stored one (`ril.evaluation.stats.Boot`, 2,000 draws, seed 0, queries ordered by id), so the pilot intervals are reproduced exactly. **Sealed order (D100):** `read_rows` and `analyse` refuse the eight repositories and the executable 377 unless given the id of an existing dev-analysis registry entry, and record it in the output.

**Decision D123 (tactical, no design content).** The module implements the protocol as fixed; points where the protocol is silent are taken in the narrowest reading, listed in `ASSUMPTIONS` of the module and as questions below, and none enters a frozen configuration. The Holm decision is read off the intervals (a contrast is rejected when its interval at its step level excludes zero; bootstrap p-values only order the contrasts), so "the interval excludes zero after Holm" and "rejected" are one statement.

**Simulations (synthetic rows; seeds 1000 to 9999; sizes and tolerances below; 500 bootstrap draws).**

| Check | Size | Result | Tolerance |
|---|---|---|---|
| Coverage of the 95% repository-clustered interval, 12 repositories of 25 queries, no repository effect | 1,000 simulations, 2 and 3 seeds | 0.912 to 0.917 | [0.89, 0.97] (Monte Carlo SE 0.7 points) |
| same, repository effect SD 1.5 points | 1,000, 2 and 3 seeds | 0.906 | [0.89, 0.97] |
| the query-level interval beside it under the same repository effect | 1,000 | 0.45 (2 seeds), 0.40 (3 seeds); 0.946 and 0.944 with no repository effect | shows why clustering is needed |
| Query-level interval, 2 repositories of 80 queries (the dev L3 case) | 1,000 | 0.937 | [0.93, 0.97] |
| Clustered interval at 8 and at 20 repositories (repository effect 1.5) | 1,000 each | 0.873 and 0.921 | not tested (8 is below the tolerance) |
| True effect 0, 1.5, 3 points | all the above | coverage is the same at the three effects because the effect is a constant shift of one cell; the effect size matters to the classification: 0 is never a reported effect, 3 always is, 1.5 about half the time by construction | |
| Holm against a hand-computed example and against an independent definition-based implementation (no statsmodels in the environment) | 200 random vectors of 1 to 8 p-values | equal to 1e-9 | exact |
| Row order, run ids, consistent seed relabelling, swapping which seed pairs with which in one arm | one panel each | identical output (or identical pooled estimates) | exact |

**Family-wise error under no effect (the check the brief asked for), 1,000 simulations each, three seeds, four confirmatory contrasts (two contrasts at two levels), Holm on the clustered percentile intervals as the protocol fixes them.**

| Repositories | Repository effect SD (points); SD of the pooled difference | Any rejection by Holm | Any reported effect (rejection and at least delta) | Beside, not implemented: cluster-robust t interval, R minus 1 degrees of freedom, same Holm |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | 0; about 0.1 | 0.224 | 0.000 | 0.051 |
| 8 | 3; 1.09 (stress) | 0.151 | 0.072 | 0.025, effect at least delta 0.011 |
| 12 | 0; 0.09 | 0.164 | 0.000 | 0.053 |
| 12 | 1.5; 0.47 (matches the design's minimum detectable difference of 1.3 at 12 repositories) | 0.100 | 0.000 | not run |
| 12 | 3; 0.92 (stress) | 0.095 | 0.032 | 0.032, effect at least delta 0.012 |
| 20 | 0; about 0.1 | 0.114 | 0.000 | 0.044 |
| 20 | 3; about 0.8 (stress) | 0.093 | 0.019 | 0.032, effect at least delta 0.012 |

**Finding: the rule as fixed does not hold the family-wise error rate at the final design's repository counts.** A percentile bootstrap over 8 to 12 whole repositories is too narrow in the tails (coverage 0.87 at 8 repositories, 0.91 at 12 at the 95% level), and Holm uses the tails (levels 0.0125 to 0.05), so the chance of at least one false rejection among the four confirmatory contrasts is 0.10 to 0.22, not 0.05. What keeps a false reported effect rare is the delta guard (0.000 where the difference is estimated as tightly as the design promises; 0.03 to 0.07 in the stress cases). The test of this property is therefore recorded as an expected failure that turns into a failure the day the rule changes (`test_false_positive_rate_after_holm_is_nominal_with_twelve_repositories`, strict), with the current rate pinned beside it. Nothing was changed, because D17 and D72 fix the interval and the correction.

**Pilot reproduction (stored per-query rows, dev repositories, exploratory; every value of the stored table equal to the module's within its rounding, largest absolute difference 0.005 in a difference or an interval end).**

| Stored summary | What is compared | Reproduced |
|---|---|---|
| E-PILOT-NOISE, rate 0.4, three seeds (`results/tables/n1/n1_effects.csv`) | oracle, FILTER-BM25, FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small and WEIGHT against RAW, and both FILTERs against the subsample, at L2 (clustered, 5 repositories, 351 queries) and L3 (query-level, 2 repositories, 155 queries): point difference and interval | 12 of 12 contrasts, e.g. FILTER-BM25 minus RAW +1.82 [+0.36, +2.86] and +1.19 [+0.25, +2.12] |
| E-MINED-PILOT, two seeds (`results/tables/mp/mp_effects.csv`) | FILTER-BM25 and the subsample against RAW at both levels | 4 of 4 (+0.93 and -0.02 for FILTER-BM25) |
| E-PILOT-NOISE seed spread and distinct examples (`n1_seeds.csv`, `n1_unique.csv`) | per-seed means and SD of six cells at both levels; distinct examples seen and the repeat flag per run | all |
| E-PILOT-NOISE dose-response, rates 0.2 and 0.4 | gain over RAW per rate; share of the oracle recovery (D58: 0.68 at L2, 0.40 at L3 for FILTER-BM25 at 0.4) | gains equal the stored ones; shares 0.68 and 0.40 |
| E-PILOT-CURVE (`results/tables/t3_curve/t3_curve_rows.csv`) | change from step 0 to step 100, mean of two seeds with the query-level and the clustered interval, and each seed's change | equal to 0.0006 (the table has three decimals): L2 +10.518 [8.318, 12.706] and clustered [4.903, 12.556]; L3 two repositories, no clustered interval |

Not reproduced because it is not a stored quantity: Holm, delta classes, claims and the fallback (they did not exist before); the pilots' minimum detectable differences (E-STORED-ANALYSIS, a different statistic). The sealed-order guard, the table provenance (every digit of a fragment is read from the summary; `SOURCES.csv` records the summary's sha256) and a byte-identical rerun are tested.

**Dead ends and surprises.** (a) My first test expected a true effect of exactly 1.5 not to be a reported effect; it is reported about half the time by construction (the point estimate reaches delta in half the simulations), so only the estimate is checked there. (b) The first call of the pilot reader gave the repository root where the run directories' parent is expected and found no runs. (c) The curve pilot's `results.json` has no seed field; the seed is taken from the config hash recorded in the entry (70f4195c is seed 0, 6c520027 is seed 1). (d) The family-wise finding above came from the first run of the check I had written to pass.

**Questions for the lead (the protocol is silent or the rule fails; each has a narrowest-reading default in the code; recommendation marked).**
1. *Interval rule for decisions.* Options: (a) keep the percentile cluster bootstrap and state the inflated family-wise error (0.10 to 0.22) with the delta guard as the protection; (b) **recommended**: decide by the cluster-robust t interval with R minus 1 degrees of freedom (measured family-wise error 0.03 to 0.05, reported effects at most 0.012) and keep the percentile bootstrap as the interval beside, recorded as a departure; (c) a wild cluster bootstrap-t (heavier, not measured). Evidence: simulation above (measured, synthetic); cheapest flip: the same simulation at the real repository counts and variance components once the final rows exist.
2. *Holm family.* The code takes the contrasts of one family on one evaluation set, both levels, and never pools sets (the eight repositories and the executable 377 each give a family; m is the number of contrasts that exist on the set). Options: (a) as coded; (b) pool the two final sets for L2; (c) fix m = 4 for every set whatever the data. Recommended (a).
3. *Families.* Coded as section 4 with D69 applied: confirmatory (FILTER-BM25 against RAW and the subsample); exploratory: SweRankEmbed-Small, then WEIGHT with CURRICULUM, then the injected cells. Confirm or regroup.
4. *Which contrasts decide the RQ3 contrast (D80, rule 7).* Coded: only the confirmatory FILTER-BM25 against RAW, positive on every final set that has both levels; exploratory contrasts never decide. Recommended as coded.
5. *Final L3.* Only the executable 377 has both levels among the final sets (the eight add L2); a positive therefore needs that set at both levels, with dev as the sign check and the leave-largest-repository-out check (django on the 377). Confirm the eight are L2-only evidence and that the executable 377 is the one L3 set.
6. *The calibration sanity rule needs a clean cell* (RAW must degrade against the clean pool). The final design has none (the clean anchor is a pilot cell, not a fresh run), so the module reports it as not evaluable. Options: (a) add a clean RAW cell on the injected pool (about 0.4 GPU-hours per seed); (b) use the pilot anchor, labelled; (c) report only the oracle-recovers half. Recommended (a), cheap.
7. *Dose-response in the final data.* The design has one noise rate (0.4), so "rises with r" is not evaluable from final rows; the r = 0.2 cells would need fresh runs (the N2 pilot is unrun). Options: (a) say not evaluable; (b) run fresh r = 0.2 cells (about 3 x 0.4 GPU-hours plus the pool score). Recommended (a) unless the report needs H1a.
8. *Small readings the module fixes* (each a flag or tie-break, none changes a verdict except the second): the "indistinguishable within one seed SD" flag is the absolute difference below the pooled SD of the two cells' per-seed means (rule 4); the leave-out check compares the sign of the point estimate (rule 3) and leaves out the repository with most queries; a dev sign that disagrees withholds the positive and the claim is then inconclusive with the reason written (section 6 has no word for it); the dev-analysis id is validated for form and as a registry row, and the final-cell evaluation script of the launch plan must apply the same function (`require_dev_analysis`) so the two cannot disagree.

**Not claimed.** Any result of RQ2; that synthetic coverage at 12 repositories carries over to the real variance components; anything about repositories beyond the counts simulated (8, 12, 20).

### Addendum 2026-11-09: the lead's rulings on the eight questions, applied before any final row (decisions D136, D137, D138, D139; DEV-040)

Evidence classes: measured (simulation, synthetic rows), reasoned. No cluster job for the analysis; CPU only.

| Question | Ruling | Applied as | Evidence class and what could flip it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 interval rule | option (b), D136: cluster-robust t with R - 1 degrees of freedom decides; Holm unchanged; the percentile bootstrap beside it in every table | `ril/analysis/cluster_t.py` (t distribution by incomplete beta function, checked against tabulated quantiles; linearised cluster variance, every query a cluster below three repositories); `holm_group` runs the step-down on the t intervals and stores the bootstrap run of the same procedure under `holm.boot`; tables show both; DEV-040 | measured; flip: the same simulation at the real repository counts and variance components once final rows exist (not a reason to change the rule after seeing a row) |
| 2 Holm family | as coded (one family on one set; never pooling the eight with the 377) | no change | reasoned |
| 3 families | as coded (section 4 with D69) | no change | reasoned |
| 4 RQ3 contrast | only the confirmatory FILTER-BM25 against RAW decides (D80 fallback) | no change | reasoned |
| 5 final L3 | the executable 377 carries both levels; a positive claim needs that set at both levels | no change | reasoned |
| 6 clean cell | add `inj_clean_raw` (D137); `calibration_sanity` is now evaluable | cell in the plan; `analyse_files` passes `CLEAN_CELL` | reasoned (0.42 GPU-hours per seed) |
| 7 dose-response | second rate 0.1 (D138): `inj_raw_r01`, `inj_filter_bm25_r01`; curve points 0, 0.1, 0.4; `dose_response` takes the clean cell, reports RAW loss at each rate and whether the loss and the FILTER gain grow, from the t interval of the difference with the bootstrap beside | cells in the plan, `DOSE_RATES`; secondary and exploratory by protocol | reasoned |
| 8 small readings | as coded (indistinguishable = absolute difference below the pooled seed SD; leave-out compares the sign; a disagreeing dev sign makes the claim inconclusive with the reason) | no change; the shared guard now exists (below) | reasoned |

**Result: the nominal rate of the new rule.** Four confirmatory contrasts under no effect (two contrasts at two levels, FILTER-BM25 effect 0), three training seeds, 25 queries per repository, repository spread of the effect 0 and 1.5 points, **1,000 simulations per cell** (seeds 5000 to 5999, 500 bootstrap draws for the beside interval, `results/logs/rq2_analysis_fwer_2026-11-09.json`, local machine, no GPU). Monte Carlo standard error 0.007. Any Holm rejection:

| Repositories | spread 0 points: t rule | percentile bootstrap | spread 1.5 points: t rule | percentile bootstrap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | 0.044 | 0.187 | 0.027 | 0.160 |
| 12 | 0.054 | 0.159 | 0.034 | 0.100 |
| 20 | 0.044 | 0.113 | 0.035 | 0.078 |

The earlier strict expected-failure test is now an ordinary passing test (`test_false_positive_rate_after_holm_is_nominal`: 1,000 simulations per cell, six cells, tolerance: at most 0.07, that is nominal plus two points, about three Monte Carlo standard errors); the old rate is pinned for the beside interval (`test_family_wise_error_rate_of_the_beside_interval_is_pinned`, bands around 0.187, 0.159, 0.100, 0.113); the coverage of the clustered interval at 12 repositories and the stress case of reported effects at tau 3 still pass (reported effects stay under 0.07). The t rule is conservative at spread 1.5 (0.027 to 0.035), as expected when the repository term is estimated from few clusters.

**One guard for the sealed order.** `ril/evaluation/sealed.py` holds `require_dev_analysis`; the analysis module (`check_sealed`, `read_rows`, `analyse`), the final-sets scoring (`final_sets.check_id`, the record loader and the index reader) and the new row converter all call it, and `tests/unit/test_sealed_order.py` fails if any of them stops calling it or carries its own copy of the id pattern. **The converter** (`ril/evaluation/final_rows.py`, `scripts/rq2_rows.py`): final-sets scoring results and the training runs' dev results to the analysis rows (one per run, cell, seed, set, level, repository, query: nDCG@10 and Recall@100 from the dense column under the primary gold reading; `n_positives` is not recorded per query and stays empty), refuses without the dev-analysis id before opening a file, tested on a synthetic final-sets result and on the stored pilot runs (the converter and the pilot reader give the same per-query values).

**Not claimed.** That the t rule is exact at the real variance components; any RQ2 result; that three noise points show a dose-response (that is read from final rows).

## E-RQ2-FINAL-CELLS: final cell configurations, per-run job scripts, evaluation by set, sealed order of the final sets; plan-only validation on two accounts. Apparatus, 2026-11-09 (decisions D124 to D134, DEV-038, DEV-039)

Status: **apparatus and plan-only validation; nothing trained, no GPU job, no model call, no change to the mining or prep jobs on account C.** Evidence class: measured (counts, scheduler accounting, free space, plan-only job outputs), projected (linear in a measured rate), reasoned (the rest). Product: `configs/experiments/rq2_final/` (36 run configurations and two templates), `ril/training/rq2_plan.py` (cell table with account, retention and the conditional cell; protocol check; budget on two bases and two evaluation scopes; per-run job graph), `ril/evaluation/eval_sets.py` and `final_sets.py` (named sets, the sealed order), `ril/bench/rq2_launch.py` (preflight, submit, records), `scripts/rq2_{plan,preflight,submit,record,final_sets}.py`, `scripts/ada/rq2f_{mined_run,injected_run,final_sets,planonly}.sbatch`, tests `test_rq2_plan.py`, `test_rq2_launch.py`, `test_eval_sets_and_final_sets.py`, `test_eval_plan.py`, `test_data_loader.py`. Outputs: `results/rq2_final/plan_only/{A,C}/`. Plan and checklist: `docs/RQ2_LAUNCH_PLAN.md` section 10.

**Decisions (the lead's rulings of prompt 62, recorded with their reason; all `provisional` until launch, none in `configs/frozen/`).**

| D | Decision | Evidence class | Cheapest experiment that could flip it |
|---|---|---|---|
| D124 | Mined-set cells (RAW, size-matched subsample, FILTER-BM25, FILTER-SweRankEmbed-Small, WEIGHT, CURRICULUM) train on account C where the store is; the injected-noise family trains on account A. Account B is not used (6-hour wall, no alias, nothing staged). The eight repositories and the built parts of the executable 377 are copied to C by byte-verified copy | measured (A has 5.1 to 5.3 GB free, C 26 GB; cache projected 5.6 GB), reasoned (calendar time) | the queue waits of the first chained jobs on C (`sacct`), one day |

A FILTER pool below 3,200 repeats examples; FILTER against the subsample is unaffected (same size by construction), FILTER against RAW carries the stated caveat; visits are never changed to compensate | reasoned; **already what the planner does** (`BatchPlanner._stream`, `PlanCounts.unique_examples_seen`), now pinned by a test | none |
| D134 | Tactical (no design content): one GPU job per run, chained per account with `afterany`; the submit script refuses without `--confirm-launch` and a passed, fresh, same-commit preflight file and never submits a queued run; the injected family reads its pool caches in place from the noise pilot's directory; the final-sets scoring enforces the sealed order in code; the launch module lives in `ril/bench/` (leakage check d); the executable-377 cost per checkpoint counts the 36 earlier instances at their whole six-folder store | reasoned, tested, validated plan-only | see Pushback |

**What was built.** (1) *Configurations:* `scripts/rq2_plan.py render` writes the 36 files from the cell table; the size-matched subsample keeps the token `filter_size` (a symbolic size cannot train: `int()` refuses it) and the run job resolves it from the prep summary and checks the hash. Every configuration is checked against `PROTOCOL_HELD` (batch 32 x (1+1), CodeRankEmbed at 512 tokens, 100 updates of the 3,000-update schedule, random repository-local negatives, learning rate 2e-5, temperature 0.05, dev roles only), seeds 100 to 102 (the plan refuses the wave seeds, D82), FILTER at K = 20 positive level, BM25 for WEIGHT and CURRICULUM on the mined set, retention on for the mined set only, no teacher equal to the base encoder. The templates equal the pilots' templates in every held-identical section (test). (2) *Run scripts:* `rq2f_mined_run.sbatch` (30 GB, node-local scratch and space guard, CUDA gate inside the run, retention directory required and absolute, size-matched subsample resolved in the job) and `rq2f_injected_run.sbatch` (24 GB, no retention), both resumable (a fixed `DATE` names the result directory, `run-batch` skips a finished run). (3) *Evaluation by set:* `--eval-set dev --home DIR` in `scripts/train.py run-batch|eval` (training-stage sets only; a final set is refused at the parser and in `for_training`); `scripts/rq2_final_sets.py` scores a retained model on the eight and on the three stored parts of the executable 377 and refuses without `--follows-dev-analysis ID`, a matching complete record that covers the run, and a model folder that matches its manifest; every score row is written with that id and the record's digest; `read_final_sets` returns nothing for another id. (4) *Cost and graph:* measured costs above; budget on both bases and both scopes; per-run graph with the conditional teacher jobs.
