**Copies (copy rule of prompt 62; local shell tar stream, SHA-256 manifest verified on C, nothing overwritten, nothing deleted).** `ril_eval/{prep,e3_cache}` (257 MB), `ril_rq3u/{prep,e3_cache}` (718 MB), `ril_rq3u_x/{prep,e3_cache}` (6 MB), 1,126 files, all identical on C, no extra file (numbered script kept outside the repository). The held-out overlap index (27 MB, 3 files) to C, identical. The last two are what rulings 1 and 9 say is copied; the built parts of the executable 377 are not named in ruling 1 but are the same kind of data and are needed by ruling 4.

**Plan-only validation (CPU; jobs 65991, 66004, 66009, 66036 on A, 65992, 66006, 66010, 66038 on C; the first three passes on each account ran earlier code, the last the merged final code; the C jobs were separate from the prep job running there).** Each pass: the plan (36 hashes distinct, fresh-noise check empty, distinct-example check illustrative at 5,800 examples and FILTER size 3,450: RAW 0.55 passes, FILTER 0.93), the render compared with the committed files (identical on both accounts), the evaluation-set check, the preflight against the real directories, a rehearsal on a synthetic work directory, the submit script's refusals. The plans on A and C are byte-identical. Real preflight: A injected passes the commit, seeds, configuration, input (read in place), memory (24,576 MB asked, 24,576 planned, ceiling 32,000), space (5.3 GB free, 3.0 needed), retention and dev-store checks and fails the registration and leakage record (open by design); C mined passes commit, seeds, configuration, memory (30,720 MB), space (26.0 GB free, 10.0 needed), retention directory and dev stores and fails registration, prep summary, inputs (the cache and feature table are the prep job's output) and the leakage record. The rehearsal passes every check on both accounts. The submit script exits 2 without `--confirm-launch` and without a preflight file, prints the graph (C: 15 jobs, 6.30 GPU-hours; with the conditional cell 18 and 7.56).

**What the validation found and fixed (dead ends included).** (a) The preflight crashed on both accounts at its first pass: `datetime.UTC` does not exist in the cluster's Python 3.10 (the linter's automatic fix had introduced it); the module now defines `UTC` once with a stated reason. (b) The torch-marked set failed leakage check (d) (`tests/leakage`, 1 failed): the launch module sat in `ril/training/` and imports the evaluation-set check, so evaluation and analysis modules were reachable from the training package; it moved to `ril/bench/` (41 passed on the cluster, job 66005). The local suite had not caught it because the leakage tests are not in `tests/unit`. (c) The injected family's pool caches (9 GB, symbolic links into earlier pilot directories) are not under the new work directory and cannot be copied (A has 5.3 GB free); the script and the preflight read them in place from the noise pilot's directory. (d) The first cost of the executable 377 (0.377) counted only the built parts; the 36 earlier instances live in the six-folder store, which `stores_for` encodes whole (0.292 projected), so the per-checkpoint cost is 0.68 and not 0.40 as the plan's placeholder said. (e) A shell expansion (`$h:ril_rq2f` read as a path modifier) created two stray directories inside the working tree on the first staging attempt; they were my own copies and were removed before any commit. (f) On the login node of C the interpreter has no numpy, so `mine_pilot.py yield` cannot run there; `state_yield` alone does.

**Mining result read for checklist item 1 (read-only, `state.jsonl` of the finished store on C).** Attempted 10,000: mined 6,230 (62.3%), no Python change 2,607, no positive 1,040, quarantined 123 (1.23%: Python 2 or other syntax 48, old file missing 26, patch missing 23, diff truncated 14, unit id rejected 8, snapshot unavailable 3, compare not found 1; **0 snapshot timeouts**), 1,102 repositories, positives (unrestricted) 54,700 at 8.78 per mined pull request; G2 (10,000 positives) met before the reading restriction. The `non_test` count and the overlap drop are the prep job's.

**Pushback (evidence class and the cheapest experiment).** One GPU job per run pays a queue wait per job. Measured wait on A: median 0.03 h, 90th percentile 2.75 h (44 jobs); the critical path of the graph is 26.1 h (1.1 days) at the median with the conditional cell dropped and 36.4 h (1.5 days) with it, but 124 h (5.2 days) and 153 h (6.4 days) if every job drew the 90th percentile (pessimistic: a tail applied to each of 48 or 56 jobs). Six-run jobs (six training jobs) would have paid it six times. `run-batch` already skips a finished run, so a six-run job loses at most the run in flight on a node loss, the same as a per-run job: the isolation of per-run jobs buys little over the resumable batch. Reasoned, not measured on chained jobs (C has one GPU job in its history). Cheapest experiment: read the waits of the first four chained jobs of the launch (`sacct`), one day; if the median passes about 1 h, switch the mined family to one job per seed (a variable `RUN_IDS` in the same script). Not changed: the ruling says one job per run.

**Not done / not known.** The prep, drop-list and dense-teacher measurement scripts (later prompts); the dev analysis and its record (`write_dev_analysis_record` is the contract); the leakage and registration records on the cluster (`scripts/rq2_record.py`); memory at full scale beyond the projected 26 GB (check `sacct` MaxRSS after the first mined job); a per-instance restriction of the stored36 part would cut up to 0.1 GPU-hours per checkpoint; the instance filter of the 377 when rows are read is not written (the analysis module's); account C's prep job was running during this work and was not touched.

**Batched job graph.** Per account and seed: C three mined jobs (five arms; six with the conditional cell, which then waits for the dense-teacher pass), A three injected jobs (nine arms: raw, filter_bm25, oracle, sub, clean_raw, filter_srs, weight, raw at 0.1, filter_bm25 at 0.1), three final-sets jobs on C (one per seed, six models each). Calendar estimate (critical path, one wait per job from account A's measured waits: p50 0.03 h, p90 2.75 h, max 8.25 h): 25.3 h (1.1 days), 58.0 h (2.4 days) and 124.0 h (5.2 days) with the conditional cell dropped; 35.5 h (1.5 days), 70.9 h (3.0 days) and 142.4 h (5.9 days) with it. Training waits are drawn six times instead of 36.

**What was built.** `rq2_plan.py`: three cells, interleaved order, `mined_updates`, `plan_runs(mined_n=)`, the noise rate of a cell checked against the configuration, equal updates across the arms of a family enforced, per-seed `job_graph`; `scripts/rq2_plan.py resolve` (the launch step: an updates record with F, N and the SHA-256 of the prep summary, resolved configurations and hashes); `ril/bench/rq2_launch.py`: `compute_updates`, `read_updates`, per-seed `submissions` (`SEED`, `RUN_IDS`, `UPDATES` exported; wall two hours per arm), `updates_computed` in the preflight, the preflight records N and the submit script refuses a mismatch, refuses without `--work` and a valid record; run scripts loop over the seed's arms through one `run-batch` call; the final-sets script takes the seed's models. Tests: plan, launch, cluster t, converter, sealed order.

**Rehearsal (CPU, nothing submitted, nothing deleted).** Re-rendered configurations equal the committed ones on both accounts (diff empty); plan, preflight against the real directories (fails exactly the prep, record and N items), preflight on a synthetic work directory (passes, N = 100), submit script: no preflight file (exit 2), no confirmation (exit 2), N not computed (exit 2). Files in `results/rq2_final/plan_only/`.

**Dead end and slip recorded.** A first loop to copy the rehearsal outputs into `results/rq2_final/plan_only/` removed the tracked files of that directory in the working folder (an unset shell variable, zsh does not split a variable on spaces); they were restored from the repository and replaced by the new outputs; no file outside the working folder was touched.

**Pushback.** (1) D141 puts the conditional cell inside the three mined jobs, so with it the mined jobs wait for the dense-teacher pass (up to 6.0 GPU-hours plus a queue wait) before any mined arm trains; the alternative is five-arm jobs now and three single-arm jobs for the conditional cell later, which costs three more waits but starts the confirmatory arms 6 hours earlier (evidence: reasoned; cheapest check: the measurement on 100 directories, which decides whether the cell runs at all). (2) A seed job holds the mined cache in memory across its arms: the MaxRSS of the first seed job against the 26 GB projection is the check. (3) The final-sets jobs are batched per seed by the same rule (not asked for): three waits instead of eighteen.

**Not done / not known.** The prep, overlap and dense-teacher scripts' outputs (F and N are not known: DEV-041 is conditional and its fill is the launch step's); the registration record on the launch commit; MaxRSS of a seed job; the dev analysis and its record; an alias for account B.

## E-RQ2-FULL-PREP: the chain from the finished mined store to the mined example cache, as job scripts, tried on a 200-directory snapshot and a large-repository probe. Apparatus and measurement, 2026-11-09 (decision D135)

Status: **done for the apparatus and the trial; the run on the whole store is a later prompt. CPU only (account C, 16 jobs, 0 GPU-hours); no model call; the store was only read.** Evidence classes: measured (the runs below), projected (linear in measured costs, no interval; the audit has a two-point reading), reasoned (the rest). Code: `ril/data_mining/full_prep.py` (final directories by state order, byte copy of a prefix of the store, audit shards and the merge, drop list on the manifest, byte checks, two-run comparison, projection), `ril/data_mining/full_prep_run.py` (the stages and their timing), `scripts/rq2_full_prep.py`, `scripts/ada/rq2_full_prep.sbatch`, `configs/rq2_prep/full.yaml`, a `merge` command in `scripts/mine_overlap_audit.py`, `--common-only` in `mine_pilot.py rank-reproduce`; tests `tests/unit/test_full_prep.py` (18). Outputs: `results/rq2_prep/` (trial, probe, projection, mining progress, cache memory). Ledger rows: account C, session RQ2P, jobs 65954 to 66093.

**Decision D135 (tactical, no design content).** (1) The example-cache reader keeps one copy of each distinct id and text (`pool_reader.read_cache`; the earlier reader stays as `read_cache_plain`): the values are equal (18 unit tests, and 3 of 3 real caches compare equal), only the number of string objects differs. (2) The cache writer fixes the gzip header time, so the same examples give the same bytes (three runs, one SHA-256). (3) The prep job asks 12 GB, not 30 GB (the finding below). (4) The audit is split by a hash of the repository name into 8 shards (a repository is never split), each shard resumable; shards run in turn. (5) Node-local scratch is not removed unless `CLEAN_SCRATCH=1`, so a job deletes nothing by default. No cell, arm, teacher, metric or claim changed.

**Snapshot (step 1).** The first 200 final mined directories by `state.jsonl` order (a row counts when its status is `mined` and its directory has its metadata file): state rows 1 to 310 of 10,000 (all 10,000 closed, none skipped), 145 repositories, copied byte for byte with their repositories' rows into `$HOME/ril_prep/trial200/store` (405,251,310 bytes in 1,236 files; every file re-hashed against the manifest). Hash of the 200 state lines `bcbe0ff6…cbf4`, of the 310-line prefix `1fdc2c73…a29c`, of the name list `7de9e92a…5db3` (`results/rq2_prep/trial200/selection.json`, `snapshot_summary.json`, `names.txt`). Materialised directories equal the store: 199 of 199 small files and rows checked by slot, and the archive, listing (without its path) and sample files of two runs hash equal.

**Chain on the snapshot (runs r1 66000, r2 66001, r3 66057, node gnode006, 12 GB asked, both readings, 10 min 49 s to 11 min 0 s each).** Peak memory is each step's own (the runner stays small; the first version did not and gave a floor of 904 MB for every step, job 65955, superseded):

| Stage | Seconds (`non_test` only, r3) | Peak MB | Output |
|---|---|---|---|
| overlap audit, 3 shards + merge + apply | 377.3 (142.6 + 173.9 + 62.9) | 1,224 | 0.29 MB records; 1 of 200 directories dropped, 2 flagged |
| drop list on the manifest | 0.0 | | 199 kept |
| materialise + byte check | 73.4 (54.8 + 13.7 + checks) | 903 | 110.9 MB of directories (node-local) |
| rank inputs (zip, listing, selection) | 36.0 | 329 | zip 64.0 MB (node-local); 188 directories, 1,177 positives, 137 repositories |
| BM25 ranks | 40.5 | 654 | 0.15 MB |
| feature table + held-out selection | 0.6 | 41 | 0 held-out rows |
| cache + FILTER size | 35.9 (32.4 + 3.3) | 603 | cache 60.3 MB; K = 20 keeps 134 of 188 examples and 356 of 1,177 positives |
| check | 4.8 | 227 | |

The `pool_aligned` reading takes 26 + 29 + 0.5 + 27 s more: 159 directories, 877 positives, cache 46.2 MB, FILTER keeps 117 of 159 examples and 295 of 877 positives. `non_test` keeps 188 of 199 directories (94.5%, Wilson interval 90.4 to 96.9; the pilot 92.5%), `pool_aligned` 159 of 199 (79.9%, 73.8 to 84.9; 84.6% of `non_test`, the pilot 80%). Held-out count of the training manifest after the drop list: 0 of 188 and 0 of 159; dropped names in the cache: 0.

**Correctness checks.** (a) Second run: 65 of 65 output files of r2 equal r1's (rank rows without their timing field; run paths aside; gzip files by content), the example caches are byte-identical (same SHA-256 in r1, r2, r3). (b) Audit shards: the records of 3 shards merged have the SHA-256 `76634e21…2c` of an unsharded audit (job 66002, 6 min 14 s), the same summary and the same drop and flag lists at every floor. (c) Pilot values: of the pilot's 1,286 rank-bearing directories, 26 are among the 200 and 93 more are in the probe; all 119 rank rows are identical (rank and rank_all of every positive, function counts) and all 446 feature rows equal. The pilot's 1,391 includes 105 directories with no `non_test` positive that have no rank row to compare. (d) `pool_aligned` and `non_test` were both produced and counted (above). (e) The overlap audit sees 26 of 605 held-out repositories (Q68 stays); it dropped 1 directory (the only directory of its repository in the 200).

**Scale probe (job 66044, 14 GB asked, 13 min 9 s).** 210 directories of apache/airflow (100), open-metadata/openmetadata (60) and azure/azure-cli (50), the largest repositories by directories and by snapshot size: 201 `non_test` directories, 810 positives, 1,527,531 candidate rows. Audit 184 s (peak 1,226 MB), materialise and check 144 s (1,317 MB), rank inputs 147 s, BM25 173 s (442 MB), cache 118 s (459 MB) and FILTER size 11.8 s with a peak of **1,674 MB** (the read of the whole cache by the earlier reader); cache 238 MB; K = 20 keeps 105 of 201 examples. Resident memory after reading the whole cache, earlier reader against shared strings (two repeats each): **1,712 MB against 295 MB** (same seconds, 14 to 15 s), and 476 MB against 352 MB on the 188-example cache of 137 repositories; the examples compare equal on the 201, 188 and 159-example caches.

**Which step needs the 26 GB, and can it be reduced.** Not a prep step: the example cache read whole by training (`load_inputs`) and by `filter-size`. The plan's 17 GB decoded is reproduced independently: the two caches hold 157 to 158 bytes per candidate row compressed and 1.12 to 1.25 KB per row decoded; the 5,860 `non_test` directories carry about 14.7 M candidate rows (0.52 of the snapshot functions in the trial and in the probe, times the mined directories' 4,819 functions on average), so **about 17 GB decoded with the earlier reader, and the cache about 2.3 GB compressed (the plan said 5.6 GB)**. Sharing equal strings gives the number of distinct rows times the same per-row size: the store holds 5,280,780 distinct rows (all files, `digests.bin`), so at most 5.8 GB if every one were in a cache and about 3.0 GB at the non-test share, **projected 3 to 6 GB** (not measured at 5,860 examples). With training 3 GB and evaluation 6 GB a run process is then about 12 to 15 GB instead of 26. The reduction changes no value.

**Finding that the plan's memory request cannot start.** Partition u22 grants at most 3,000 MB per CPU (`MaxMemPerCPU`), so a request of 30 GB is allocated 11 CPUs (`MinCPUsNode=11`), above the account's limit of 10 CPUs: job 66022 waited with reason `QOSMaxCpuPerUserLimit` on an otherwise empty queue until cancelled. A 12 GB and a 14 GB request were allocated 6 CPUs. The largest request that can start is 30,000 MB, which holds all 10 CPUs. `scripts/ada/rq2f_mined_run.sbatch` asks 30G (and `rq2_plan.MEM_REQUEST_MB` and the preflight check hold 30,720 MB for it), so the 18 mined runs as written would not start. Not changed here (another branch's script and constants); the remedies are `--mem=30000M`, or, with the shared reader, a request near 16 GB, and the constants and the preflight check move with it. Evidence: measured (the job, `scontrol`). Cheapest experiment: a one-minute job with the intended request.

**Mining progress (read only; the run finished before this task started: jobs 64593 and 64594 COMPLETED, last summary 2026-11-08 20:54 IST).** 10,000 attempted: mined 6,230 (62.3%; pilot 69.55%), no Python change 2,607, no positive 1,040, quarantined 123 (1.23%): changed file unparseable 48 (Python 2 syntax 21, other 27), old file missing at base 26, patch missing 23, diff truncated 14, unit id rejected 8, snapshot unavailable 3, compare not found 1; **0 snapshot timeouts**. 54,700 positives unrestricted (8.78 per mined), 1,102 repositories seen. Store: `pr` 0.103 GB, `cas` 1.035 GB, `listing` 0.724 GB. The plan's 3.7 GB projection counted the listing directory (already complete at 2,985 rows) as growing; the distinct-row store grows by quarter of the state log by 947, 61, 19 and 9 MB (567, 235, 124 and 87 new repositories), so it saturates; the mean snapshot size per mined directory is flat (4,992, 4,455, 4,970, 4,864 functions) while the first 200 mined have 3,783. Mining time 9.55 h in two sessions.

**Projection to the whole mined set (6,230 directories; linear, no interval; the audit's second reading is a two-point fit through the trial and the probe).** Per stage for the `non_test` reading on 2 CPUs: audit 1,578 s (two-point) to 14,967 s (linear per function); materialise and check 1,350 to 2,913 s; rank inputs 1,398 to 1,428 s; BM25 about 1,605 s (cost proportional to functions); feature table 5 to 22 s; cache and FILTER size 1,238 to 1,426 s; check 96 to 189 s. **Whole chain about 2.0 h (two-point audit) to 6.3 h (every stage linear), the `pool_aligned` reading about 0.7 h more**; the job asks 8 h. Peak memory is decided by the largest repository (google-cloud-python has 870,248 distinct rows; the pilot's 946,825-row repository took 4.8 GB in the audit and 3.4 GB in materialise) and by the cache read (3 to 6 GB shared). Disk: mined cache about 2.3 GB, `pool_aligned` about 1.8 GB, ranks and tables under 2 MB, node-local view about 4.4 GB (145 bytes per snapshot function on both runs) and archive about 2.5 GB (82 bytes per function), lost with the node; account C has 24 GB free (27 GB used of 50), against the plan's 3.7 GB store and 5.6 GB cache. Counts for the full set from the trial (one draw of 200, exploratory): `non_test` about 5,860 directories (5,600 to 6,010), `pool_aligned` about 4,950 (4,570 to 5,260), about 31 of 6,230 dropped (6 to 170).

**Dead ends and surprises.** (a) The first run's memory column was contaminated by the runner's own verification in process (a child inherits its parent's high-water mark); verification and the check moved to their own processes, the first run (`trial200_a`) and a cancelled second run (`trial200_b`) stay on C, superseded. (b) A job asking 30 GB never started (above). (c) `sbatch --export` splits on commas and spaces, so the readings and stage lists use colons. (d) The first comparison of two runs flagged four JSON files: they hold the run's own paths and the hash of a listing whose first field is the archive's path; the archive, the listing without its path and the sample file are compared by hash instead. (e) The linear projection by directories overstates the audit (11,834 s against the two-point 1,578 s) because the audit costs per distinct row more than per directory; the probe, whose directories repeat a few repositories' rows, made that visible. (f) The first compound command that wrote a copy script and ran the A-to-C copy was denied, and a write into the local notes folder was refused; the copy script was written to the session scratch space and run from there (held-out index 27 MB and the pilot's rank and feature files, SHA-256 manifest taken on A and verified on C, nothing overwritten).

**Not run / not known.** The chain on the whole store (a later prompt: the command sequence is in `docs/RQ2_LAUNCH_PLAN.md` section 10.6 and stage 3); peak memory and wall time of the audit and the cache read at full size are projections; the dense teacher cell and its cost; whether the shared reader's 3 to 6 GB holds at 5,860 examples; the `pool_aligned` and `non_test` counts of the whole store; the answer to Q68 (26 of 605 held-out repositories are visible to the audit).

## E-A-OWNER-ITEMS: graph results against the same-file control, stacked expansion, test and configure edges on L3, and the other open items of workstream A. Measured (dev only, exploratory), 2026-11-09

Status: **done for items 1, 2 and 3; items 4 and 5 not run or partly run, reasons below.** Done by the lead while the owner of workstream A is away (expected back about Nov 10 to 11), on the separate branch `b/a-owner-items` from main; nothing of the owner's files or branches was edited (`docs/status/person_a.md`, `docs/experiments/A.md` and `results/ledger_a.md` are untouched). No decision is taken and no frozen setting or ADR changes; proposals are at the end. Evidence class: measured on the development repositories (five at L2, two at L3), exploratory; the Verified instances were not read. Configurations `configs/experiments/g1_cre.yaml` (hash 0e1c4c7b8e81) and `g1_qwen3.yaml` (hash 1a8bede79b2d), fixed before any score was computed.

**Gate.** The ladder entry (E-RQ1-LADDER) was not on main at the start, and not after the 6 hours of polling (18 polls, every 20 minutes); the ladder table was therefore not available while this was run. The ladder entry merged afterwards (E-RQ1-LADDER, D145, below it in this file); its reading agrees: same-file expansion is a gain on dev, the typed rung is not. This entry tests the same rungs on the score-level cell with more edge sets and the stacked list, and is not part of the ladder table. Items 1 and 2 were done against the stored dense scores and caches, as the fallback allows.

**Setup (items 1 and 2, one job).** Job 66561 (account A, CPU, 4 CPUs, 3 min 14 s, 39 MB peak, node-local nothing needed): the cell of E-GRAPH-ROLE unchanged (score-level blend, C 5, gamma 0.7, two hops, weight 0.5, cap 50, 3 draws) on the stored caches and dense scores of the 355 dev instances with dense scores, for both first stages of E-GRAPH-ROLE (RRF(BM25, CodeRankEmbed 1,024 tokens) k 100, primary; RRF(BM25, Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B 512 tokens) k 20, beside). L2 nDCG@10 on 351 queries in 5 repositories, L3 Recall@100 on 155 queries in 2 repositories (openlibrary, qutebrowser); gold `units_all_nomod` (D18 primary), `units_all` beside it. Code `ril/evaluation/g1_stacked.py`, tables `ril/analysis/g1_tables.py`, wrapper `scripts/g1_run.py`, job `scripts/ada/g1_stacked.sbatch`, tests `tests/unit/test_g1_stacked.py`. Interval: the cluster-robust t interval of D136 (repository-clustered with R - 1 df at L2; **at L3 two repositories fall below the three-repository rule, so it is the query-level t interval**), with the clustered and the query-level percentile bootstrap beside it (`results/tables/g1/*_contrasts.csv` carry all three). A variant counts as a graph gain only if the t interval lies above 0 against its same-file control; otherwise "not a graph gain". **Regression control in the job:** the graph, same-file, next-best and random-same-file arms of the stored edge set (`core`) equal the stored E-GRAPH-ROLE rows on every one of 355 instances (largest absolute difference 0.0; the job stops above 1e-9). Query-level rows: `results/tables/g1/g1_{cre,qwen3}_v2_query_rows.csv`; 710 evaluation files with SHA-256 in `results/logs/g1_run_files_2026-11-09.sha256`; environment `results/logs/g1_env_2026-11-09.txt`. The tables were written by the job and re-written once from the stored evaluation files (a label change only; same values). Two earlier submissions failed before computing (a log path relative to the submit directory, a missing stemmer package in the new environment); a first run without the recency arm (job 66552) gave the same values for every shared arm.

**Item 1: the stored graph against both controls (CodeRankEmbed first stage; points; t interval).**

| contrast | L2 nDCG@10 (5 repositories) | L3 Recall@100 (2 repositories) |
|---|---|---|
| graph minus same-file by score (control 1) | +0.72 [-0.20, +1.64]; clustered bootstrap [+0.13, +1.27] | -0.98 [-3.30, +1.35]; bootstrap [-2.54, +0.45] |
| graph minus random units of the seeds' files (chance, size-matched) | +1.08 [-0.65, +2.81] | +0.88 [-1.39, +3.15] |
| graph minus next best first-stage units (chance, size-matched) | +2.55 [+1.24, +3.86] | +11.78 [+9.49, +14.06] |
| same-file minus next best units | +1.83 [-0.10, +3.76] | +12.75 [+10.38, +15.12] |
| same-file minus first stage | +1.23 [-1.46, +3.92] | +13.20 [+10.77, +15.63] |

Qwen3 first stage: graph minus same-file +0.57 [-1.17, +2.32] (L2) and -1.01 [-2.97, +0.96] (L3); graph minus random same-file +1.02 [-1.45, +3.48] and +2.02 [-0.07, +4.11]. The L3 number reproduces E-GRAPH-ROLE's dev value (-1.0).

**Item 1, stacked expansion (the flip test, Q30).** Budget M = size of the stored-graph expansion (about 49 units); the reference is the same-file list of M units; in the stacked list the last N (10 and 25) are replaced by the best non-contain neighbours of the seeds (`nc`: import, invoke, inherit), keeping the values of the positions they take; chance controls fill the same positions with the next best units or with random units. About 8 of 10 replacements lie outside the seeds' files (`results/tables/g1/*_sizes.csv`). Stacked minus the unreplaced same-file list, CodeRankEmbed: L2 +0.01 [-0.31, +0.32] (N = 10), +0.11 [-0.36, +0.59] (N = 25); L3 **-0.35 [-1.38, +0.68] (N = 10), -2.46 [-4.06, -0.85] (N = 25)**, per repository at N = 25 -1.50 (openlibrary) and -3.51 (qutebrowser). Qwen3: L3 +0.03 [-0.81, +0.87] and -2.91 [-4.65, -1.17]; L2 +0.00 [-0.09, +0.10] and -0.15 [-0.30, -0.00]. The same swap against the chance fills: stacked is above both at L3 (N = 25: +4.47 [3.07, 5.88] over the next best units and +6.12 [4.77, 7.48] over random units, CodeRankEmbed): neighbours beat chance units for those positions and lose to the same-file units they displace.

**Item 2 (E-GRAPH-L3, dev, two of the three repositories).** `coretc` (stored graph plus tests and configures) against the same controls: graph minus same-file -1.08 [-3.39, +1.23] at L3 (core alone -0.98), +0.70 [-0.19, +1.59] at L2; adding the two families moves nothing (the stacked `nctc` rows equal `nc` within 0.01). Tests and configures alone (`tc`, about 11 units per query against 49 for the stored graph, controls size-matched): L3 minus same-file -1.60 [-3.18, -0.02] (CodeRankEmbed) and -1.55 [-3.21, +0.11] (Qwen3); minus the first stage +1.01 [+0.08, +1.94] and +0.22 [-0.71, +1.16]; L2 minus same-file -1.96 [-3.80, -0.12] and -2.80 [-5.28, -0.32]. Stacked `tc` is below same-file at L3 (N = 25: -1.19 [-2.14, -0.24]; only about 11 neighbours exist, the rest of the swapped positions are same-file filler). **Reading: no edge set tried (stored, with tests and configures, tests and configures alone, non-contain only, stacked at two replacement sizes) beats the same-file control on dev at either level, with either first stage; each is reported as "not a graph gain".** The third repository (ansible, 83 instances) has a cache but **no dense scores** (encoding needs a GPU job, not allowed by this brief; about 130,000 distinct chunk texts in its L2 corpus at 109 texts per second would be roughly 0.3 to 0.5 GPU-hours at 512 tokens, reasoned from D107's rate, 1,024 tokens costs more); the Verified-six was not read.

**Recency prior (A3, cheap part).** The E-RECENCY dev setting (lam 0.2, tau 30 days, fixed, not re-tuned) on the same blended first stage, with the same ages shuffled over the units as chance (3 draws): CodeRankEmbed L3 minus first stage +4.06 [+2.56, +5.57], minus shuffled ages +7.15 [+5.46, +8.85], **minus same-file -9.14 [-11.80, -6.47]**; L2 minus first stage +0.67 [-1.49, +2.83]. Qwen3: L3 +3.58 [+2.01, +5.15] over the first stage and -7.68 [-10.27, -5.09] against same-file; L2 -1.08 [-5.05, +2.89]. Recency helps L3 over the unexpanded first stage and is far below same-file expansion.

**What it says (measured on dev, exploratory; two L3 repositories, so the L3 intervals are query-level and the repository variation is not covered).** (1) Against the same-file control the stored graph is level at L2 (+0.7, interval includes 0) and level to slightly below at L3 (-1.0); against random units of the same files it is not distinguishable (+0.9, +1.1 at L2). (2) The L3 gain of every structural variant over the first stage is the file-level gain: same-file alone gives +13.2 [10.8, 15.6]. (3) Tests and configures add nothing to the stored edges. (4) Stacking graph neighbours over same-file units does not help and costs 2.5 to 2.9 points at L3 when a quarter of the budget is swapped. **Not shown:** that the graph carries no signal anywhere (two L3 repositories; Verified-six not read; ansible missing; hop-decay order only; one budget; the replaced positions take the lowest values, an order not varied). More resolved edges are therefore not a result: the downstream number does not move against the control.

**Item 3 (A2b and A2c leftovers).** *A2c p95 repeats (done).* Job 66564 (account A, CPU, node gnode004, 40 CPUs visible, Python 3.10.12, 29 min): the edge store written and loaded 31 times per check level, so the nearest-rank p95 is the 30th of 31, not the maximum. Checkouts are the cluster's own (commits in `results/logs/a2c_p95_commits_2026-11-09.txt`; **different commits and hardware from the owner's laptop run, so the two tables are not compared row by row**; click is not on the cluster account and is absent). Hash-checked load p50 / p95: requests 0.038 / 0.046 s, flask 0.056 / 0.067 s, pytest 0.30 / 0.31 s, django 2.96 / 3.08 s (224,155 edges); every round trip equal. Tables `results/tables/a2c_p95_edge_store.csv`, log `results/logs/a2c_p95_edge_store_2026-11-09.json`. The p95 is within 3 to 20% of the p50 on every repository. *The two ledger rows without a config hash (`a2b-resolver-pilot-static`, `a2b-resolver-pilot-dynamic`): not recoverable, statement left as it is.* The two logs hold no configuration; the option sets are code constants (`PILOTS` in `ril/bench/resolver_pilot.py`) on which no hash function was applied at run time, and the build-config hashes of the five checkouts sit in the neighbouring row; a hash computed now would be a new number, not the recorded one. *Remaining open in A2b* (what the owner's entry lists): a second traced test suite for the dynamic reading and inter-procedural argument typing; not run (the first needs a traced suite and checkouts of a second repository; cost not measured).

**Item 4 (field weights and unit-level BM25): not done.** The dev repositories' unit texts at their base commits are not stored: the E3 caches hold arrays only, the E1 records hold positives, the encoded stores hold vectors. Rebuilding them is the cost: parsing the 355 dev instances without ansible is at most 3,622 CPU-seconds (about 1.0 CPU-hour, the summed `seconds_total` of their cache builds, which includes graph and history; ansible would add 3,531 s), then one unit-level index per instance for each BM25 variant (one factor at a time, E1 default as control) and the fused cell again with the stored unit-level dense scores. About half a day of work including the job; stopped by the brief's rule. The Verified instances' rows and the lead's 377 caches were not touched.

**Item 5 (A3 and A4 as in the owner's plan): inventory plus what was cheap.** A3 (comparisons the plan lists, with the same candidate budget): no expansion, graph as stored, same-file by score, same-file random, non-contain only, reach share by family: stored (E-GRAPH-ROLE) and reproduced above; recency prior: run above; tests and configures: run above; the exact way the graph enters the fusion (edited list, fused list, scored list): only the scored-list form was run here, the other two exist in the E3 and X2 code but were not put against the same-file control (about an hour of code and 5 minutes of CPU; not run). A4 (the ablation table with MRR, Recall@10, latency columns, the candidate stack file and paired adjacent-row intervals): not run; the ladder entry covers the rung ladder and had not merged, so building a second table would duplicate it; the MRR and Recall@10 columns, per-row latency columns and the candidate stack configuration file remain (cost: the stored dense and fused rows are reused, CPU only, about a day).

**Cut or skipped.** Verified-six reading (not allowed by the brief); ansible dense scores (GPU); item 4 (inputs absent); A3's two other fusion forms; A4; A5 and A6 (not in this brief). **Dead ends.** Two failed submissions (above); the old environments on account A have no numerical packages (site packages emptied; new environment `~/ril_aitems/venv310`, a login node is Python 3.9 and cannot run it, use a compute node).

**Commands.** `rsync -a ril scripts configs ada:ril_aitems/ && cd ~ && sbatch ril_aitems/scripts/ada/g1_stacked.sbatch` (job 66561); `sbatch ril_aitems/scripts/ada/a2c_p95.sbatch` (job 66564); `python3 scripts/g1_run.py tables <eval dir> <prefix>`. Ledger rows by account letter in `results/logs/ada_jobs.csv`; headline rows in `RESULTS.md` (`g1-*`, `a2c-p95`). CPU only, 0 GPU-hours.

**Questions for the lead.** (1) Report the structural rung of the ladder as "not a graph gain" on this dev evidence, with the L3 two-repository caveat? (recommended: yes, with the Verified-six read once by the final-sets scoring). (2) Should ansible be encoded (about 0.3 to 0.5 GPU-hours, reasoned) to get the third L3 repository? (recommended: only if the ladder needs a third L3 repository). (3) Should same-file expansion be named as the expansion option that is measured (dev: +13.2 at L3 over the first stage), instead of the typed graph? Proposal only; no frozen setting changes.

**Message for the owner.** "While you were away the lead ran your open items on a separate branch (b/a-owner-items; nothing of yours was edited). The stored graph, tests and configures, non-contain edges and stacked expansion do not beat the same-file control on dev at L2 or L3, with either first stage (intervals above); the recency prior helps L3 over the first stage but is far below same-file. The A2c p95 repeats were run on the cluster (31 repeats). Field weights were not run (no unit texts at the dev base commits; cost above); A3's other fusion forms and A4 remain with you. Merge main and read E-A-OWNER-ITEMS."


## E-RQ1-LADDER: the RQ1 signals ladder on the development set, with latency per configuration. Measured (dev only, exploratory), 2026-11-09 (decision D145)

Status: **done.** Done by the lead while the owner of workstream A is away; the owner's remaining items are listed at the end and were left alone. Evidence class: measured on the development set from the stored caches and stored dense scores; exploratory; the Verified folders were not read (the Verified-six numbers below are stored values of earlier entries, already read, shown as stored). Configurations: `configs/experiments/rq1_ladder_cre.yaml` (sha256 e4110148259a, evaluation hash f1c913370655), `rq1_ladder_qwen3.yaml` (b985dea33c89, 2c1268f7187d), `rq1_ladder_latency.yaml` (f6245d7690c1); seed 0; instance cache hash 70b481631de4 (the E3 cache with the D18 readings, 521 instances of which 355 development instances have stored dense scores). Hardware: account A, CPU job, node gnode004 (Xeon E5-2640 v4, 4 cores), 0 GPU-hours; peak resident size 56 MB, 80 s per encoder; jobs 66554 and 66559 (superseded, see dead ends) and 66562 (final). Code `ril/evaluation/rq1_ladder.py`, `ril/analysis/rq1_ladder.py`, `ril/analysis/rq1_ladder_tables.py`, `scripts/rq1_ladder.py`, `scripts/ada/rq1_ladder.sbatch`; tests `tests/unit/test_rq1_ladder.py` (synthetic rows, the stored latency log and the stored result rows). Stored outputs: `results/tables/rq1_ladder_{cre,qwen3}_{summary,compare,sizes,query_rows}.csv`, `rq1_ladder_regression.csv`, `rq1_ladder_latency.csv`; `results/logs/rq1_ladder_run_files_2026-11-09.json` lists the 710 evaluation files with SHA-256 and the hashes of the configurations, the latency log and the cache.

**Decision D145** (reasoned; the cheapest experiment that could flip each part is named; all four parts were fixed before any ladder row was read). (1) *The ladder*: rung 1 dense alone; rung 2 plus BM25 by RRF; rung 3 plus same-file expansion; rung 4 plus typed graph expansion over non-contain edges; rung 5 plus the recency prior; on the same queries, the same candidate set (every unit of the snapshot) and the same expansion budget (cap 50, C 5, gamma 0.7, two hops, graph weight 0.5, the stated cell of E-GRAPH-ROLE, no new tuning). Rung 3 is the same-file expansion size-matched to the stored graph; rung 4 is an alternative expansion of the same budget read against the same-file control of its own size, the same-file chance control (same files, random order, three draws) and a top-M control (the next best units with no file constraint), all taking the graph's score values in order; it is not stacked on rung 3 (the stacked test is the owner's item and is not run here). A typed-graph gain that does not exceed the same-file control is reported as "not a graph gain". Rerank is not built and is reported as not run (ADR-009 open). Encoder: CodeRankEmbed untrained (the working default of D30) at 1,024 tokens with RRF k 100, the dev selection of E-DENSE-FUSION; Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B at 512 tokens with k 20 is run as a labelled secondary because E-GRAPH-ROLE holds it. (2) *Metrics and intervals*: L2 nDCG@10 and L3 Recall@100 with the other metric beside it (D71), mean over queries, reading `units_all_nomod` (D18) with `units_all` in the stored tables; the decision interval is the cluster-robust t interval over repositories with R - 1 degrees of freedom (D136; every query its own cluster below three repositories, so L3 on two repositories is query-level and marked), the percentile bootstrap over whole repositories (D17) beside it; adjacent rungs are paired on the query. (3) *Latency* from stored rows only: per stage p50, p95, p99 at the stated host, query-set id and index size; points per added millisecond for each rung; a stage without a row is listed as not measured and nothing is interpolated or re-measured. (4) *Dev only*; every rung carries its evidence class; no new Verified reading. Flip experiments: for (1) the stacked expansion at the same budget (owner's item, about an hour of code, ten minutes of CPU); for the recency setting a Verified reading of the fused stage (not allowed here, D100 and D12); for the encoder choice the dense scores of the third dev repository (ansible, GPU, new encoding).

**Inventory (written before the run).** The four registry rows E-RQ1-DENSE, E-RQ1-DENSE-BM25, E-RQ1-DENSE-STRUCT and E-RQ1-ALL had no entry text behind them (status "planned"); the evidence sat in the entries below. All values are dev, `units_all_nomod`, CodeRankEmbed 1,024 tokens, over the same 351 L2 queries (5 repositories) and 155 L3 queries (2 repositories).

| Rung and metric | Stored entry and configuration | Stored value (L2 nDCG@10 / L3 Recall@100) | Comparable with its neighbours? | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 dense alone | E-DENSE-FUSION, `x2_first_dev` abf0ba1e339e | 18.24 / 34.00 | yes, same cache and queries as rung 2 | none |
| 2 plus BM25 by RRF | E-DENSE-FUSION, k 100 | 24.04 / 43.00 | yes | none |
| 3 plus same-file expansion | E-GRAPH-ROLE, `s1_graph_role_cre` d457d884b16e, source `c` | 26.33 / 55.28 | **no**: blended into the score-level fusion (alpha 0.4, 25.10 / 42.08), not into the RRF of rung 2 | rung 3 on the RRF first stage |
| 4 plus typed graph (non-contain) | same entry, source `e` | (graph minus same-file control -6.12 at L3, score-level base) | **no**, as rung 3 | rung 4 on the RRF first stage |

The same 355 development instances have stored dense scores for CodeRankEmbed at 512 and 1,024 tokens and Qwen3 at 512 tokens (0 unit-count mismatches with the cache); the third dev L3 repository (ansible, 83 instances, 72,340 units at most) has cache files but no dense scores and is outside every number here (dense encoding of its units is GPU work: not done). Unit texts at each base commit are not on account A beyond the E3 cache, so unit-level BM25 variants (field weights) cannot be added: not done.

**What was run and what was only assembled.** *Run* (job 66562, CPU, two encoders, 355 instances each): rungs 3 to 5 on the RRF first stage with their controls, rung 5 and its controls, the regression control of the earlier entries, and the query-level rows. *Assembled from stored files*: rungs 1 and 2 (recomputed, equal to the stored values), the latency rows, the Verified-six values quoted below, and the BM25 tokenizer finding. The regression control inside the job recomputes BM25, dense alone, RRF, the score-level first stage, the stored graph and the same-file control at the score level for both encoders, dev, both levels: **24 of 24 values equal the stored ones within 0.005 points** (stored values are rounded to two decimals), so the engine is the one of E-DENSE-FUSION and E-GRAPH-ROLE.

**Result: the ladder (CodeRankEmbed, dev; mean over queries, cluster-robust t interval; L3 intervals are query-level, two repositories).**

| Rung | L2 nDCG@10 | L3 Recall@100 | L3 nDCG@10 | gain over its predecessor, L2 / L3 (t interval) | reading |

| 2 plus BM25 by RRF | 24.0 [12.2, 35.8] | 43.0 [39.4, 46.6] | 42.8 | +5.8 [+2.0, +9.6] / +9.0 [+6.6, +11.4] | gain at both levels |
| 3 plus same-file expansion | 25.8 [12.5, 39.2] | 55.2 [50.9, 59.4] | 48.1 | +1.8 [+0.1, +3.5] / +12.2 [+9.9, +14.5] | gain; at L2 the interval barely excludes 0 |
| 4 plus typed graph (non-contain) | 25.3 [12.6, 38.0] | 48.2 [44.3, 52.0] | 45.9 | +1.3 [-0.3, +2.8] / +5.2 [+3.5, +6.9] over rung 2 | gain over rung 2 at L3 only; **not a graph gain** (below) |
| 5 plus recency prior | 25.2 [11.1, 39.3] | 49.7 [45.7, 53.6] | 47.0 | -0.1 [-1.6, +1.4] / +1.5 [+0.4, +2.6] over rung 4 | L3 only, two repositories |

The level values carry wide intervals because the five development repositories differ widely (clustered t, 4 degrees of freedom); the paired differences are much tighter. The bootstrap beside each difference is in `rq1_ladder_cre_compare.csv`; it agrees with the t interval about excluding 0 in every row except these (CodeRankEmbed, L2): rung 4 minus rung 2 (t [-0.3, +2.8], bootstrap [+0.2, +2.2]), rung 4 minus the top-M control (the same pair), RRF minus BM25 (t [-1.9, +7.6], bootstrap [+0.3, +6.3]), and the score-level stored graph minus the same-file control (t [-0.2, +1.6], bootstrap [+0.1, +1.3]); with five repositories the bootstrap is the narrower of the two, as D136 found. Qwen3 (secondary): L2 24.7, 28.3, 29.5, 28.6, 29.5 and L3 46.7, 47.1, 58.7, 51.0, 53.4 for rungs 1 to 5; rung 2 over rung 1 is +3.7 [+0.4, +6.9] at L2 but +0.4 [-1.0, +1.7] at L3 (Qwen3 alone is already level with the RRF there), rung 3 over rung 2 is +11.6 [+9.2, +13.9] at L3 and +1.2 [-0.3, +2.8] at L2.

**Result: the typed graph against its controls (paired differences, points; CodeRankEmbed, Qwen3 in brackets where it differs in reading).**

| Difference | L2 nDCG@10, t [bootstrap] | L3 Recall@100, t [bootstrap] |
|---|---|---|
| rung 4 minus same-file control (size-matched, same score values) | -0.5 [-2.0, +0.9] [-1.6, +0.2] | -5.6 [-8.0, -3.3] [-7.9, -3.5] |
| rung 4 minus same-file chance (random order in the same files) | -0.5 [-2.5, +1.6] [-1.8, +0.6] | -3.6 [-5.9, -1.3] [-5.8, -1.4] |
| rung 4 minus top-M control (no file constraint; equals the first stage) | +1.3 [-0.3, +2.8] [+0.2, +2.2] | +5.2 [+3.5, +6.9] [+3.6, +6.8] |
| stored graph (contain included) minus same-file control | +0.6 [+0.1, +1.1] [+0.2, +0.9] | -0.6 [-2.7, +1.5] [-2.7, +1.4] |
| score-level stored graph minus same-file control (E-GRAPH-ROLE cell, reproduced) | +0.7 [-0.2, +1.6] [+0.1, +1.3] | -1.0 [-3.3, +1.3] [-3.3, +1.2] |

Mean expansion sizes (primary reading rows of `rq1_ladder_cre_sizes.csv`): 47.8 units at L2 and 45.3 at L3 for the non-contain graph and its controls, 49.6 and 49.1 for the stored graph. Gold units inside the expansion at L3: non-contain graph 6.0, its same-file control 9.5, same-file chance 6.9, top-M 6.1. For Qwen3 rung 4 minus the same-file control is -1.1 [-2.5, +0.4] at L2 and -6.6 [-9.1, -4.0] at L3; the stored graph minus it is +0.2 [-0.4, +0.9] and -0.9 [-2.9, +1.1].

**Result: the recency prior (paired, on the stated lam 0.2, tau 30 days).** On the RRF first stage: L2 +0.4 [-1.5, +2.3], L3 +2.0 [+1.1, +2.8]; on the typed rung: -0.1 [-1.6, +1.4] and +1.5 [+0.4, +2.6]; on the same-file rung: -0.1 [-1.9, +1.7] and +0.4 [-1.1, +2.0]. Qwen3: L3 +3.0 [+1.5, +4.4] (RRF), +2.4 [+1.2, +3.5] (typed), +0.1 [-1.6, +1.8] (same-file); L2 all within [-1.6, +2.1] around +0.2 to +0.9.

**Latency per configuration (stored rows; `rq1_ladder_latency.csv`; a stage is the latest warm row of its query set).** Index of 35,860 units (django-size), dense stages on the GPU node (RTX 2080 Ti, gnode044, commit 928edb7): query encode (CodeRankEmbed, 1,024 tokens) p50 / p95 / p99 18.07 / 19.28 / 22.15 ms, flat exact dense search 0.267 / 0.268 / 0.278 ms; BM25 0.202 / 0.269 / 0.307 ms and graph expansion (stored graph, all families, hop decay) 1.140 / 2.135 / 2.854 ms on the Apple M3 (commit bba24b0); the same dense stages on the CPU node (gnode006, 4 threads of a Xeon E5-2640 v4): query encode 411.5 / 1,265.2 / 2,555.2 ms, dense search 4.956 / 5.123 / 5.256 ms. At the smaller scale (dense search at the dev median snapshot, BM25 and graph on flask, 974 units): 0.113 / 0.117 / 0.131 ms, 0.040 / 0.045 / 0.047 ms and 0.149 / 0.259 / 0.561 ms. Not measured, no row: rank fusion, same-file expansion, recency prior; there is also no end-to-end row for any dense configuration (the stored end-to-end row is BM25 to assembled context, 11.44 / 17.28 / 23.65 ms at django size). Sum of stage p50 over the rungs (GPU node plus M3, so not an end-to-end percentile, and a lower bound because three stages are missing): rung 1 18.34 ms, rung 2 18.54, rung 3 18.54, rung 4 19.68, rung 5 19.68. Points per added millisecond (paired gain over the predecessor on the stage path divided by the p50 of the stages the rung adds; rung 4 branches from rung 2): rung 2 +28.7 (L2) and +44.5 (L3), partial because the fusion stage has no row; rung 4 +1.1 and +4.5 on the graph-expansion row; rungs 3 and 5 not measured. The reading is Pareto, not a ranking by the per-ms column: the query encoder costs about 18 ms on the GPU and about 411 ms on the CPU node, two orders of magnitude above every other stage, so on these rows the signal choice is nearly latency-irrelevant next to the encoder; the dense search is the only stage whose cost depends visibly on the index size (0.113 to 0.267 ms on the GPU, 0.40 to 4.96 ms on the CPU).

**What the evidence supports, per rung (measured on dev, exploratory, one stated cell, zero-shot encoders).**
1. *Dense alone*: below BM25 at L2 and L3 for CodeRankEmbed (18.2 and 34.0 against 21.2 and 37.5: -2.9 and -3.5), above it for Qwen3 (24.7 and 46.7: +3.5 and +9.2; E-DENSE-FUSION).
2. *Plus BM25 by RRF*: **gain** at both levels for CodeRankEmbed (+5.8 and +9.0, intervals above 0); for Qwen3 a gain at L2 (+3.7) and **no distinguishable change at L3** (+0.4). The BM25 here is the reference form: the tokenizer gain of E-A1-TAKEOVER (+4.6 / +5.1 alone at L2) shrinks to +1.0 / +1.1 inside RRF(BM25, CodeRankEmbed) at L2 with clustered intervals that include 0, so it does not belong in this ladder as a separate rung.
3. *Plus same-file expansion*: **gain** at L3 (+12.2) and a small gain at L2 that only just excludes 0 (CodeRankEmbed +1.8 [+0.1, +3.5]; Qwen3 +1.2 [-0.3, +2.8], not distinguishable). At L3 the interval is query-level over two repositories: it says the effect is not query noise in those two repositories, not that it holds across repositories.
4. *Plus typed graph (non-contain)*: **not a graph gain.** At L3 it is below the size-matched same-file control (-5.6 [-8.0, -3.3]) and below same-file chance (-3.6), and above only the top-M control (+5.2), which is the first stage itself; at L2 it is level with the control (-0.5 [-2.0, +0.9]). The stored graph (contain included, which never leaves a file) is level with the same-file control at L3 (-0.6 [-2.7, +1.5]) and +0.6 [+0.1, +1.1] above it at L2 for CodeRankEmbed only (+0.2 [-0.4, +0.9] for Qwen3); that L2 difference is small, its interval just excludes 0, and it did not repeat with the second encoder, so it is not read as a graph gain either. This repeats E-GRAPH-ROLE (score-level base) on the RRF first stage: the earlier statement stands that there is no evidence that typed edges add beyond same-file expansion, with the interval above; it is not proof that they add nothing (two repositories at L3, a cell tuned on the same development set for the graph, no stacked test).
5. *Plus recency prior*: a gain of about 1.5 to 3 points at L3 on the RRF and typed stages (query-level, two repositories), nothing distinguishable at L2, and nothing on the same-file rung. Caveat that limits the reading: the setting (lam 0.2, tau 30 days) was chosen on this same development set in E-RECENCY for a BM25 first stage, so the dev number is optimistic; the confirmation of E-RECENCY on Verified was for BM25 only. Read as: a candidate signal that is worth a Verified look only after the final configuration is fixed, not a result.
6. *Rerank*: not run.

**Verified-six numbers already stored (already read; not recomputed, not new).** E-DENSE-FUSION, CodeRankEmbed 1,024: BM25 18.0 / 31.8, dense alone 17.9 / 33.6, RRF 23.0 / 38.0 (L2 nDCG@10 / L3 Recall@100); E-GRAPH-ROLE (score-level first stage, 37.8 at L3): stored graph +5.7 [2.5, 9.2], same-file by score +7.7 [4.0, 12.2], non-contain only +0.4 [-1.5, 2.3]; stored graph minus same-file -2.0 [-5.2, 0.8] (clustered [-6.4, 0.6]); non-contain minus same-file -7.3 [-11.6, -3.5]. The ordering is the one of the dev ladder.

**The state of the configuration freeze (nothing frozen here; a proposal for the lead).** `configs/frozen/` holds only its README; the freeze checklist in `docs/ROADMAP.md` ("Before week 6") is open, and no test fails on a changed frozen file because none exists. What the consumers actually use today: *RQ2 evaluation* (the rq2_final templates, `ril/evaluation/checkpoint_eval.py` on the X2 machinery): dense alone and RRF(BM25, dense) at **k 60**, role dev, 512-token training length (D31), flat exact index, no expansion; *RQ3 contexts* (D107, D108, D102, D103): stored scores of the untrained encoder at 512 tokens, BM25, dense alone and RRF at **k 100**, 200 candidates, assembly with unit cap 1,000, class units in header form, approximate tokenizer, budgets 8,000 and 16,000, no expansion; *this ladder and E-GRAPH-ROLE*: CodeRankEmbed at 1,024 tokens, k 100. So three values disagree: RRF k (60 in the architecture document and the RQ2 evaluation, 100 in RQ3 and the dev selection), the truncation (512 for training and RQ3, 1,024 for the dev-selected dense stage), and whether any expansion is on (no consumer uses one). Stored evidence for settling the first two (E-DENSE-FUSION table, dev, CodeRankEmbed): RRF at k 60 against k 100 is 24.03 against 24.04 (L2 nDCG@10) and 42.66 against 43.00 (L3 Recall@100) at 1,024 tokens, and 23.56 against 23.45 and 42.63 against 42.72 at 512 tokens; RRF at 512 against 1,024 tokens (k 60) is 23.56 against 24.03 and 42.63 against 42.66. Proposal (reasoned, not decided; the flip experiment is a dev-only table of those cells with intervals, from stored scores, about ten minutes of CPU): freeze RRF k 60 and 512 tokens, the training length and the architecture document's value, which costs at most half a point at L2 on dev; freeze with no graph expansion and no recency prior, because the typed rung is not a graph gain and the recency setting is dev-selected and unconfirmed; leave same-file expansion by score as a candidate for the lead (Q30), since it is the one expansion that shows a gain over the first stage at L3, but not part of the RQ2 held-identical block unless the lead adds it (the held-identical block in `docs/08_experimental_design.md` is not edited here). A freeze record must contain: the base checkpoint and revision, seed policy, architecture, pooling, batch, learning rate and schedule, warm-up, weight decay, optimiser updates, temperature, maximum length for training and for query-time encoding, evaluation set and metric code version, index type, RRF k and candidate count, the BM25 variant and parameters (reference form, k1 1.5, b 0.75, `provisional: ADR-004`), whether each expansion and prior is on, the context assembly version (ADR-014, Proposed), the code commit and the SHA-256 of every file in the directory, and a latency snapshot of the frozen stages (p50, p95, p99 per stage, host, query-set id, index size, cold and warm), plus a test that fails when a file of the directory changes after the hash was recorded (not written). The first two lines of that record, the commit hash and the latency snapshot, can only be filled when the freeze happens.

**Not run / not known.** Rerank (not built); the stacked expansion; E-GRAPH-L3 (tests and configures edges); a third dev L3 repository (needs dense scores of ansible, GPU encoding); unit-level BM25 variants and field weights (need unit texts at each base commit); latency of rank fusion, same-file expansion and recency prior, and any dense end-to-end row (a harness stage per item, about an hour of code each, CPU; not built here: the ladder uses stored rows only); the dev repositories' own index sizes for BM25 and graph expansion; any Verified reading of the ladder (reserved for the final-sets scoring, D100, D12); seeds (the retrieval stack is deterministic given the stored scores; random draws of the chance control use three draws keyed on the query, so the chance rows carry draw noise that no interval here includes); the cell and the recency setting were tuned on this development set earlier, so every dev number for rungs 3 to 5 is optimistic in absolute terms (the controls share the cell, so the differences against them are not inflated by it).

**Dead ends.** (a) The first job (66554) failed on the Qwen3 configuration (an unquoted colon in a YAML value); the CodeRankEmbed half ran. (b) The regression control caught a real mistake in the first version: the score-level control seeded its expansion from the score-level ranking, while E-GRAPH-ROLE seeds from the RRF ranking and blends into the score-level fusion; the graph controls differed by 0.2 to 0.4 points (not reproduced), no ladder row was read from that version, the seeds were separated from the blend base, the evaluation version was bumped and the job re-run (66562: 24 of 24 reproduce). Jobs 66554 and 66559 are superseded. (c) Cluster-side `python3` on the login node is 3.9 without numpy, so the inventory runs inside the batch job.

**What remains for workstream A (copied from the rulings of round 4; done or open as of this entry).** (1) Merge `main` into `a/rq1` and push: the engineer and the owner, open. (2) Every graph result in RQ1 against the same-file control: done on dev for the RRF first stage here (rung 4, the stored graph and the score-level cell), the Verified-six values are as stored; the stacked expansion flip test of Q30: open (the owner's items were being worked on in parallel on branch `b/a-owner-items`; check that branch's entry before repeating anything). (3) E-GRAPH-L3: open. (4) A2b and A2c remaining work: open. (5) Field weights and unit-level BM25, then the fused cell again; one reading of the Verified six with the single final setting, only after the lead says so: open. (6) A3 and A4: the dev-set ablation table with latency columns is this entry (A4 as far as stored rows allow); A5 (rerank) and A6 (end-to-end latency profile) open.

**Where it goes in the report.** The RQ1 slot (gap-map rows 8 and 9 of `docs/REPORT_GAPS.md`): five table fragments named rq1_ladder (ladder with latency), rq1_ladder_pairs (paired differences and controls), rq1_ladder_latency (per-stage rows) and the Qwen3 pair rq1_ladder_qwen3 and rq1_ladder_pairs_qwen3, produced by `python scripts/report_tables.py --include rq1_ladder --out-dir <directory>` from the stored tables (every digit checked against a stored file; the default build and the report are unchanged, because the report inputs every fragment of its generated directory and was not edited here: the lead adds the inputs when the slot is filled). The prose to write under them is the per-rung reading above, the Pareto paragraph and the freeze paragraph; the claim stays "exploratory, development set, zero-shot encoders, Python only".

**Reproduce.** `rsync -a ril scripts configs ada:ril_rq1/` then `sbatch ril_rq1/scripts/ada/rq1_ladder.sbatch` on account A (CPU, resumable); copy `ril_rq1/rq1_ladder_v2/` back; `python scripts/rq1_ladder.py tables <repository root> 2026-11-09 cre=<dir>/cre qwen3=<dir>/qwen3 --env job=66562`; `python scripts/report_tables.py`. Rows `rq1-ladder-*` of `RESULTS.md`.

## E-FREEZE-AND-LATENCY: freeze records per consumer, the three missing stage latencies, and unit-level BM25 variants inside the fusion. Measured (dev only, exploratory), 2026-11-10 (decision D148)

Status: **done** (the three items; every item that could not be completed is listed under "Not done"). Done by the lead while the owner of workstream A is away. Evidence class: *freeze records* are records of stored settings (read, not measured), with measured dev sensitivity beside them; *latency rows* are measured on the Apple M3 of the existing BM25 and graph rows, CPU only; *unit-level BM25* is measured on the development set, exploratory. No GPU job, no Verified evaluation, no push.

**Decision D148** (one decision, three parts; reasoned for the freeze form, measured for the sensitivity it rests on). (1) *Freeze by consumer, no unification.* The RRF k is 60 in the RQ2 evaluation (templates of `configs/experiments/rq2_final/`) and 100 in the RQ3 contexts (`configs/experiments/rq3_contexts_v1.yaml`) and in the dev selection and ladder (`configs/experiments/rq1_ladder_cre.yaml`); the truncation is 512 tokens in the first two and 1,024 in the third. Stored dev rows (E-DENSE-FUSION, `results/tables/x2_first_dev.csv`, units_all_nomod, CodeRankEmbed) put the three settings within 0.59 points of each other on L2 nDCG@10 and 0.37 on L3 Recall@100 at most: RQ2 evaluation (512, k 60) against the dev selection (1,024, k 100) 0.48 / 0.37, against the RQ3 contexts (512, k 100) 0.11 / 0.09; RQ3 contexts against the dev selection 0.59 / 0.28. (The "at most 0.5 points" stated when the work was asked for holds for the RQ2 evaluation against either other setting and is 0.59 for the RQ3 contexts against the dev selection; the larger value is the one stated.) Unifying would change the hash of the 45 registered RQ2 run configurations (the cell table digest is part of the record), re-make the RQ3 contexts and the pilot built on them, for a gain that no stored row shows. Each consumer's setting is therefore frozen as its own named record; no consumer's code or configuration was changed. No expansion and no recency prior in any record (rungs 4 and 5 are not gains over same-file expansion on dev, E-RQ1-LADDER; same-file expansion by score stays an unfrozen candidate, Q30). (2) *A record* holds the consumer, the configuration as the consumer stores it (read from the stored files), the encoder (revision pinned) and the BM25 variant, the resolver version (`own-ast-resolver/v0`, D117 pending), the assembly rule and budgets (D102, D103), the sensitivity above and a latency snapshot; a test fails when a record, its sources, the 45 run configurations or a recorded latency row changes. (3) *Latency* of the three stages without a row is measured, not interpolated; the unit-level BM25 variants are a dev-only run with a rule fixed before it. Evidence class: freeze form reasoned only; sensitivity measured (stored, exploratory, dev). Cheapest experiment that could flip it: the final-sets scoring of k 60 and k 100 on the eight and the executable 377 once the dev analysis is recorded (not allowed before, D100, D12). Conditions to revisit: the lead wishes a single k for all consumers (then a new record and a re-registration, not an edit).

**Item 1: the freeze records.** `configs/frozen/{rq2_evaluation,rq3_contexts,rq1_dev_selection}.json` and `MANIFEST.json`, written by `scripts/freeze_records.py write` (logic `ril/bench/freeze.py`, code commit recorded in each file), verified by `tests/unit/test_frozen_records.py` (the manifest digest is pinned in the test; nine tests, including tamper cases: a changed record, a changed consumer configuration, a changed run configuration, a missing latency row, a stray file, an attempt to rewrite). All three consumers were located; no consumer is listed as not found. Tie to the stored hashes: the RQ3 file hash equals the hash stored beside the built contexts (`results/rq3_contexts/config_hash_63792.txt`, job 63792), the dev-selection file hash equals the one in the ladder's run record (`results/logs/rq1_ladder_run_files_2026-11-09.json`), and for RQ2 the record holds the `config_hash` of each of the 45 rendered configurations and the cell table digest, with the evaluation section identical in all 45. Latency snapshot of each record: the stored rows of the stages on its path (query encode at the consumer's truncation on the GPU node, flat dense search, BM25, rank fusion, and context assembly for the RQ3 record) at the django size (35,860 units) and at the small size (BM25, fusion and assembly on flask, 974 units; dense search at the dev median snapshot, 5,195 units); nothing is missing. Two limits of the records: values that carry `provisional` in their consumer keep it (`provisional_in_consumer`: ADR-001, ADR-002, ADR-006 for RQ2; D102, D103, ADR-006 and ADR-014 (Proposed) for RQ3), so the records are a freeze of what each consumer uses and not a promotion of those values to final (the repository's rule is that provisional settings do not enter `configs/frozen/`; these records state the provisional labels they carry and the lead decides whether they stand until the ADRs close); and the RQ2 record covers the evaluation side and the held-identical training sections as the templates state them, it does not replace the registration and leakage records the launch needs (plan section 11.5).

**Item 2: latency of rank fusion, same-file expansion and the recency prior.** Stages `rrf_fusion`, `same_file_expansion`, `recency_prior` (taxonomy extended in `ril/bench/latency_harness.py`, stand-in stages in `stub_stages.py`, `specs/latency_harness.md`; code `ril/bench/fusion_measure.py`, tests `tests/unit/test_fusion_measure.py`). Each stage calls the function the ladder itself uses. *Inputs*: BM25 scores of the real index of the snapshot and its query set (the 200 queries of `retrieval-<repo>-v1`), stored CodeRankEmbed dense score vectors of 25 development instances (1,024 tokens) resized to the unit count by drawing stored values with replacement (seeded), ages from the snapshot's own git history (35,859 of 35,860 units have one at django size). *What a row covers*: `rrf_fusion` from the two score vectors to the 100 best units of the fused ranking (both whole rankings by full sort, the reciprocal rank sum at k 100, selection); `same_file_expansion` from a fused ranking (prepared) to the 50 units of the files of the top 5; `recency_prior` from a fused score vector to the 100 best units after the prior (the prior over every unit is recomputed per call, so this is an upper bound for a service that caches it). Machine: Apple M3, 8 cores, 8 GB, host as the existing BM25 and graph rows; code commit 90a609f; query-set ids `fusion-django-v1` and `fusion-flask-v1`; 200 queries, 5 warm-up calls discarded, 3 repeats, so 595 warm samples per stage and 200 cold; p50 / p95 / p99 in ms; configuration hashes 560ce0a99e53 (django) and e3d3ddfb0566 (flask). Memory: peak resident size 57 MB (flask) and 472 MB (django) measured with the 200-query cache (four score vectors per query), against the local limit of 8 GB.

| Stage (warm) | django, 35,860 units, p50 / p95 / p99 | flask, 974 units, p50 / p95 / p99 |
|---|---|---|
| rank fusion | 5.044 / 5.359 / 5.564 | 0.124 / 0.134 / 0.139 |
| same-file expansion | 0.412 / 1.919 / 3.079 | 0.063 / 0.144 / 0.161 |
| recency prior | 2.039 / 2.099 / 2.163 | 0.066 / 0.071 / 0.074 |

*Regression control* (a stage that already has a row, re-measured in the same run and process: BM25 on the same query sets): django 0.197 / 0.261 / 0.300 ms against the stored 0.202 / 0.269 / 0.307 (-2.6% at p50); flask 0.046 / 0.051 / 0.054 against the stored 0.040 / 0.045 / 0.047 (+15% at p50, 6 microseconds; a second run gave 0.046 again, so the flask row of 2026-10-17 was faster than this machine is today by that margin and the new flask rows are read with it in mind). Both pass the harness threshold (50% and 0.5 ms). *What the rows say* (measured): rank fusion costs 5.0 ms at the django size, twenty-five times BM25 and the largest stage after the query encoder, because it sorts two whole rankings of 35,860 units; the recency prior costs 2.0 ms for the exponential over every unit and a third sort; same-file expansion is 0.4 ms at p50 but its p99 is 3.1 ms (the walk down the ranking until 50 units of the seeds' files are found). A selection that avoids the full sorts would cut the fusion row, but it changes how ties are broken unless written with care and no consumer's code is changed here; it is listed as an optimisation candidate to land before the RQ2 runs start (the latency requirement in `docs/07_latency_and_profiling.md`), not done.

*The ladder's latency column* (`results/tables/rq1_ladder_latency.csv`, rebuilt from the log by `scripts/rq1_ladder.py latency` through the table builder; the selection file `configs/experiments/rq1_ladder_latency.yaml` points the three stages at the new query-set ids). The path is a sum of stage p50 values, not an end-to-end percentile: dense stages on the GPU node plus BM25, fusion and expansion on the M3. Path p50 at django size, rungs 1 to 5: 18.34, 23.58, 23.99, 24.72, 26.76 ms (earlier lower bounds: 18.34, 18.54, 18.54, 19.68, 19.68); at the CPU-node scale 416.42, 421.67, 422.08, 422.81, 424.85 ms; at the small scale 18.18, 18.35, 18.41, 18.50, 18.56 ms. Points per added millisecond (paired gain over the predecessor divided by the p50 the rung adds; rung 4 branches from rung 2) at django size: rung 2 +1.1 (L2 nDCG@10) and +1.7 (L3 Recall@100) for 5.246 ms (BM25 and fusion), rung 3 +4.4 and +29.6 for 0.412 ms, rung 4 +1.1 and +4.5 for 1.139 ms, rung 5 -0.05 and +0.7 for 2.039 ms. The earlier "partial" values for rung 2 (+28.7 and +44.5) are superseded: they left the fusion out and it is the dominant cost of that rung. Caveats of the path: (a) the stored graph-expansion row (`graph_expansion`) times BM25 followed by the expansion, so BM25 is counted in rung 2 and again in rung 4 (the expansion alone, `graph_expand_2hop`, is 0.463 ms p50 on the same query set and machine; the ladder keeps the row it selected before, so rung 4 and rung 5 are 0.2 ms high on the path); (b) the fusion row includes the sorts the later stages reuse; (c) stages are on different hosts and index scales (the small scale pairs the dense search at 5,195 units with flask-size rows); (d) the dense scores of the fusion row are stored values resized to the unit count, so the row measures the cost of the operation, not a real query's list. The reading is Pareto as before: with the encoder at 18 ms on the GPU (411 ms on the CPU node) the signals' own costs are small, but the fusion at django size is the first non-encoder stage that is visible next to it.

**Item 3: unit-level BM25 variants and field weights (D119), development set, CPU only.** Code `ril/evaluation/unit_bm25.py`, `ril/analysis/rq1_unit_bm25.py`, `scripts/rq1_unit_bm25.py`, job `scripts/ada/unit_bm25.sbatch` (4 CPUs, no GPU), configuration `configs/experiments/unit_bm25_dev.yaml` (sha256 03d05b0c5c56, the rule below written before any variant row was read), tests `tests/unit/test_unit_bm25.py`. The instance cache holds scores, not texts, so the unit texts were rebuilt at the base commit of every development instance with the parser of the cache (working clones on node-local scratch, sorted unit id order) and the *control must reproduce the cache's stored BM25 scores bit for bit* on every level or the instance is not evaluated. Cells, one factor at a time against the control (reference tokenisation of the unit source, k1 1.5, b 0.75): identifier splitting (whole identifier kept: `tok_split`; dropped: `tok_split_noorig`), k1 0.9 and 2.0, b 0.4 and 0.9 (the extremes of the A1-DEV grid), integer field weights added to the body (`fw_name2`: name twice; `fw_path1`: path once; `fw_pilot`: name twice, signature once, path once). The fused cell: RRF of each variant with the stored CodeRankEmbed 1,024-token scores at k 100 (the dev selection), no expansion, no recency prior. Rule (fixed before the run): intervals are the cluster-robust t interval over repositories with the repository bootstrap beside it (L3 has two repositories, so it is query-level and marked); the best variant is the non-control cell with the highest mean L2 nDCG@10 of BM25 alone; it "changes the fusion" if its fused L2 nDCG@10 difference to the control fusion has a t interval above 0; the same is reported at L3 Recall@100 and for every other cell. Job 67069, account A, node gnode002 (Xeon E5-2640 v4), 4 CPUs, 16 min 12 s, 56.6 CPU-minutes, peak resident size 4.4 GB of the largest process (12 GB requested for four shards; no per-shard estimate from measured numbers was made beforehand, only the cache build's earlier runs; it fitted), 0 GPU-hours; code commit 12838c9. 355 instance files evaluated (354 with a located gold unit; 351 L2 queries in 5 repositories and 155 L3 queries in 2, the same sets as the ladder); the 83 instances of the third development Level 3 repository (ansible) have no stored dense scores and are listed, not evaluated (the job ends with exit 2 because of them). **Regression: 508 of 508 levels reproduce the stored BM25 scores exactly, and the three control keys equal the ladder's stored values on the same queries (BM25 21.1704 / 37.473, RRF 24.0359 / 42.996, dense 18.2427 / 34.0024 at L2 nDCG@10 / L3 Recall@100; 6 of 6 equal, difference 0).** Stored: `results/tables/unit_bm25_{summary,compare,regression,query_rows}.csv` (query rows with the hashes of every table and of the 355 evaluation files in `results/logs/unit_bm25_run_files_2026-11-10.json`).

| Cell (dev, units_all_nomod) | BM25 alone, L2 nDCG@10 minus control, t [bootstrap] | fused, L2 nDCG@10 minus control, t [bootstrap] | fused, L3 Recall@100 minus control, query level, t |
|---|---|---|---|
| tok_split (best by the rule) | +3.03 [-0.15, +6.20] [+1.09, +5.24] | -0.29 [-1.74, +1.16] [-1.33, +0.45] | +2.75 [+1.59, +3.90] |
| tok_split_noorig | +2.19 [-0.65, +5.02] [+0.49, +4.03] | -0.28 [-2.01, +1.46] [-1.52, +0.43] | +2.67 [+1.47, +3.87] |
| k1 0.9 | +0.69 [-0.71, +2.09] | +0.37 [-0.33, +1.07] | -0.32 [-0.89, +0.26] |
| k1 2.0 | -0.80 [-1.74, +0.15] | -0.40 [-1.45, +0.65] | -0.19 [-0.57, +0.20] |
| b 0.4 | +0.59 [-1.50, +2.69] | +1.14 [-0.89, +3.17] [+0.07, +2.90] | -1.26 [-2.07, -0.44] |
| b 0.9 | -1.50 [-2.43, -0.57] (loss) | -0.79 [-2.01, +0.43] | +0.07 [-0.29, +0.42] |
| fw_name2 | +0.59 [+0.13, +1.05] | +0.36 [-0.15, +0.86] | -0.25 [-0.51, +0.02] |
| fw_path1 | +1.87 [+0.25, +3.48] | +1.37 [-0.07, +2.81] [+0.75, +2.72] | +1.08 [+0.40, +1.76] |
| fw_pilot | +2.56 [+0.61, +4.51] | +1.79 [-0.10, +3.68] [+0.92, +3.58] | +0.95 [+0.26, +1.65] |

Levels (mean, t interval): L2 nDCG@10 BM25 control 21.17 [9.46, 32.88], fused control 24.04 [12.24, 35.83]; best-rule cell tok_split 24.20 alone and 23.75 fused; L3 Recall@100 control 37.47 and 43.00, tok_split 45.28 and 45.74. **Does anything change inside the fusion? By the rule, no, at Level 2.** The best variant by the rule (identifier splitting) gains +3.0 over the control on its own at L2 (repository-clustered t interval [-0.15, +6.20]: not distinguishable; the bootstrap [+1.09, +5.24] excludes 0, as D136 found it narrower with five repositories), and inside the fusion it is -0.29 [-1.74, +1.16]: the alone-gain does not survive the fusion, as the tokenizer gain of E-A1-TAKEOVER at chunk level did not (+4.6 alone, +1.0 fused). No cell has a fused L2 nDCG@10 interval above 0: the field-weight cells are the nearest (`fw_pilot` +1.79 [-0.10, +3.68], `fw_path1` +1.37 [-0.07, +2.81]; their bootstraps are above 0 and their t intervals just include it). Beside that primary reading, at L2 Recall@100 inside the fusion `fw_path1` is +2.93 [+0.88, +4.98] and `fw_pilot` +2.64 [+0.82, +4.45] (gains with clustered intervals; the splitting cells +1.6 and +1.4, intervals including 0), and at Level 3 (two repositories, query level, so only a statement about those queries) splitting is a gain inside the fusion at Recall@100 (+2.75) and nDCG@10 (+3.03), the path field +1.08 and the pilot weights +0.95, and b 0.4 a loss (-1.26). Reading (evidence: measured on dev, exploratory, five repositories at L2, two at L3): the variants that help BM25 alone are mostly absorbed by the dense signal at the top of the ranking (nDCG@10 at L2), but identifier splitting and the path field raise candidate recall, which matters for the RQ3 contexts (200 candidates, a token budget) more than for the RQ2 retrieval metrics. The best-by-rule variant is therefore reported as **not a gain inside the fusion on the primary metric at L2**; the recall readings are candidates for a Verified look only after the lead decides the final setting (not done here: no Verified reading, D100, D12). Nothing was selected for a frozen configuration by this run: the records keep the reference form of BM25 and say that no unit-level variant is frozen. Limits: the cells were chosen from the A1 pilot and the A1-DEV grid (nothing tuned on the test folders); the path and name fields are added to the body text, so a function whose name is in its own body counts it again; ten cells and two levels were read, so a single interval that just excludes 0 is not a finding; the dense scores are the 1,024-token stored ones of the ladder, not a re-encoding.

**Not done / not known.** A latency row for a dense end-to-end configuration, rerank (not built), the stacked expansion, a Verified reading of anything, the third development Level 3 repository (needs GPU encoding), and a cheaper top-k fusion (an optimisation candidate, see item 2). The ladder fragments (`rq1_ladder*.tex`) rebuild from the stored tables through `python scripts/report_tables.py --include rq1_ladder --out-dir <directory>` (checked: 20 fragments); they are opt-in and not tracked, as before. The per-commit stub latency rows of that requirement were not appended: the `10-latency` hook is not installed (as in the commits since 2026-10-23), and a check run on a copy of the log passed (20 comparable rows, no regression). The stored ladder run record still names the earlier hash of the latency selection file (f6245d7690c1), which changed with this entry; the new hashes are in the freeze records and in the log.

**Dead ends.** (a) The first freeze record carried the encoder's full hub identifier, which the provenance check rejects; the records now hold the model name and the pinned revision, and the identifier stays in `ril/retrieval/dense_encoder.py`, where it is built the same way. (b) The first freeze records were written before the code was committed, so their code commit was not the one that wrote them; they were rewritten before anything was committed. (c) Main carried committed merge markers in `RESULTS.md` (lines 453, 457, 462 of the file before this entry); only the three marker lines were removed, every row was kept.

**Where it goes in the report.** Rows 8 and 9 of `docs/REPORT_GAPS.md` (updated); the latency paragraph of the RQ1 slot (the three new rows and the Pareto reading), the freeze paragraph (the records and the 0.59-point sensitivity) and the unit-level BM25 sentence (identifier splitting and the path field raise candidate recall, the alone-gain does not survive on nDCG@10); the claim stays "exploratory, development set, zero-shot encoders, Python only".

**Reproduce.** Latency: `python -m ril.bench.fusion_measure <django clone> django/django django --dense-glob "<stored dense score files>/*.npz"` and the same for flask (`pallets/flask flask`); then `python scripts/rq1_ladder.py latency .`. Freeze: `python scripts/freeze_records.py verify .`. Unit-level BM25: `rsync -a ril scripts configs ada:ril_ub/`, `sbatch ril_ub/scripts/ada/unit_bm25.sbatch` on account A (CPU, resumable), copy `ril_ub/unit_bm25_v1/` back, `python scripts/rq1_unit_bm25.py tables . 2026-11-10 <dir>`. Rows `freeze-*`, `latency-fusion-*`, `unit-bm25-*` of `RESULTS.md`.
